== Introduction

This document describes the setup procedures to be performed before operating the DCI System, targeting the tasks performed by DCI Operators (see <<sec_glossary>>). +
Note: Tasks performed by Tenant Operators are not covered in this document.

Completing the procedures in this document results in the DCI Management Cluster (DCI Manager and DCI Physical Infrastructure) operating normally, with tenant logical infrastructure provisioning enabled. Specifically:

* Completing Chapters 3 and 4 finishes the new construction.
* For server Expansion, execute only Chapter 4.
* For operational tasks after construction, refer to the Operations Manual.

Note: Basic knowledge of Linux and Kubernetes is required to read this document.

[[sec_glossary]]
=== Glossary

This glossary contains definitions of DCI-specific, Kubernetes, CDI, networking, and infrastructure terms used throughout the DCI-2.0 System.

.Glossary
[cols="1,1,4", options="header"]
|===
|Category |Term |Description

.14+|DCI-2.0-Specific Terms
|DCI Controller
|The collective name for software that controls and manages the DCI System. It runs on the DCI Manager, DCI Physical Infrastructure, and DCI Tenant Logical Infrastructure components.

|DCI System
|The entire system, including the software, hardware, and network equipment of the DCI Manager, DCI Physical Infrastructure, and DCI Tenant Logical Infrastructure.

|DCI Manager
|The functionality that manages the DCI Physical Infrastructure and DCI Tenants. It provisions and deletes tenants and constructs and manages logical infrastructure.

|DCI Tenant
|An environment for Tenant Operators configured on the DCI Physical Infrastructure. It consists of the DCI Tenant Logical Infrastructure and DCI Applications.

|DCI Tenant Logical Infrastructure
|Infrastructure that the DCI Manager constructs and manages as a logically independent environment for each tenant. It includes the DCI Application execution environment and common management functions.

|DCI Application
|An application constructed and operated by a Tenant Operator. It consists of Kubernetes (K8s) Pods.

|Application Image
|The collective name for container images of User-Specific Applications and Standard-Provided Applications.

|User-Specific Application
|An application registered and used by a Tenant Operator.

|Standard-Provided Application
|An application registered by a DCI Operator and shared by multiple tenants.

|DCI Management Server
|A server that is not included in Physical Resources and is used for DCI Manager and DCI Physical Infrastructure software. (A server not used for the DCI Tenant Logical Infrastructure as a Logical Server) 

|DCI Management Cluster
|A Kubernetes cluster consisting of DCI Management Servers and groups of VMs on those servers, where DCI Manager and DCI Physical Infrastructure software run together.

|DCI Provider
|A service provider that deploys, installs, and operates the DCI System.

|DCI Operator
|One of the actors described in this document. As a department of the DCI Provider, it operates the entire DCI System.

|Tenant Operator
|One of the actors described in this document. It uses a DCI Tenant to operate DCI Applications.

.5+|Kubernetes
|Kubernetes
|Container orchestration software, abbreviated as K8s.

|K8s Cluster
|A Kubernetes container execution environment. It consists of a Control Plane and Nodes.

|K8s Control Plane
|A group of components that manages the entire K8s Cluster.

|K8s Node
|A Node that runs workloads in a K8s Cluster.

|Node
|A Logical Server connected to a Logical Network and available for use as a K8s Node.

.4+|CDI
|CDI
|Composable Disaggregated Infrastructure. An infrastructure platform that manages Devices individually and flexibly combines them to construct servers.

|Composed Server
|A Logical Server constructed by CDI by combining Devices in a Device Pool.

|Compute Server
|A server used in a CDI environment by dynamically assigning resources such as GPUs, NICs, and SSDs.

|PG-CDI
|Abbreviation for Primergy CDI, a CDI solution provided by Fujitsu Limited.

.8+|Network
|Physical Network
|The collective name for network equipment consisting of L2 switches, photonic-electronic convergence L2 switches, and other equipment.

|Logical Network
|A network configured on a Physical Network and logically isolated between tenants.

|Application Data Network
|A network for main-signal and application-data communication.

|CNI Network
|A network for Kubernetes CNI communication.

|Host Network
|A network for DCI Physical Infrastructure host communication.

|Manager Network
|A network for managing the DCI Physical Infrastructure and DCI Manager.

|Physical Network
|A DCI Physical Infrastructure component and the collective name for network equipment consisting of L2 switches, photonic-electronic convergence L2 switches, and other equipment.

|Logical Network
|A network used by the DCI Tenant Logical Infrastructure and created logically on the Physical Network of the DCI Physical Infrastructure. It is logically isolated from other DCI Tenants.

.9+|Infrastructure/Resources
|DCI Physical Infrastructure
|The hardware and management functions that constitute the DCI System.

|Physical Resources
|The collective name for COTS Servers, CDI-managed Devices, and their controllers.

|COTS Server
|A preassembled commercial off-the-shelf server.

|Device
|A server component such as a CPU, memory, GPU, storage device, or NIC.

|Resource Pool
|A collection of Physical Resources that are not assigned to a DCI Tenant.

|COTS Server Pool
|A collection of COTS Servers that are not assigned to a DCI Tenant.

|Device Pool
|A collection of CDI-managed Devices that are not assigned to a DCI Tenant.

|Logical Server
|The collective name for COTS Servers, Composed Servers, and VMs.

|Configuration Management DB
|A database that centrally manages information about every DCI component, including the physical infrastructure, logical infrastructure, and network settings of the entire DCI.

.5+|I/O Device
|Card
|The collective name for PCIe Cards.

|RDMA
|Remote Direct Memory Access. A technology that enables direct communication between memory locations over a network.

|GPUDirect RDMA
|A technology that enables direct communication between GPU memory locations.

|SR-IOV
|A technology that virtualizes and assigns Physical Devices.

|VF
|A virtual Device generated by SR-IOV.

.3+|External Systems
|External Systems (DCI Operations, etc.)
|External systems such as OSS/BSS involved in DCI Operations.

|External Systems (Tenant Operations, etc.)
|External systems used for tenant operations and application development.

|External Systems (Cloud)
|Cloud systems that integrate with DCI Applications.

.2+|Other
|Components
|The collective name for OSS and DCI-2.0 deliverables deployed to the DCI Manager, DCI Physical Infrastructure, and DCI Tenant Logical Infrastructure in this system.

|Developed Items
|Software developed based on the requirements definition.
|===

=== Prerequisites

==== Hardware Installation Requirements

Hardware installation procedures for system deployment are outside the scope of this document; only requirements are described.

The following are requirements for hardware installation:

* (Req. 1) Physical environment (COTS Servers, CDI devices, etc.) has been installed and initially configured based on <<recommended_hardware_specs>>, and network devices have been installed and initially configured based on <<network_configuration>>, so that the physical environment is connected to and can communicate on all networks.
* (Req. 2) COTS Servers and CDI devices have their IPMI ports configured, L2 NW devices have their management ports configured, and all can be operated remotely.
* (Req. 3) The network is separated into manager and tenant (host, CNI, application data) segments, and VLAN IDs and subnets for tenants are defined in advance for the number of tenants the system supports.
* (Req. 4) The subnets used by tenant NICs (host, CNI) (separated by VLAN ID) are L3-routed between Manager NW and tenant NW on L3SW or router, for the number of subnets defined in Req. 3 in advance.
* (Req. 5) The L2SW devices to which tenant NICs (host, CNI, application data) are connected must have no VLAN ID configured on the ports to be operated from DHRC (default VLAN ID must also be removed).

==== Expected Values as Prerequisites

The following external services and environments are assumed to be prepared in advance.

* NFS server has been constructed
** export configuration includes no_root_squash
* DNS server is available, any DNS server can be reached via Manager NW, and name resolution on the Internet is possible
* NTP server is available and time synchronization is possible for each server
* MinIO has been constructed as backup storage (for DCI Management Cluster and DCI Tenant infrastructure)

==== Prerequisites Verification Method

Before starting construction, verify that the following requirements are met.
Verification is performed through deliverables such as management tables and connectivity checks to actual equipment.

* (Req. 1) Ping succeeds from Manager NW to the manager interface of each COTS Server and Compute Server
* (Req. 2-1) It is possible to log into the IPMI port/L2NW device management port via Web or CLI
* (Req. 2-2) A user with sudo privileges for control from DHRC is created on each SW
* (Req. 3) Tenant VLAN IDs and subnet list are organized as a management table
* (Req. 4-1) Routes to the subnets defined in Req. 3 are configured on the L3SW or router
* (Req. 4-2) Routes between tenant VLAN IDs defined in Req. 3 are NOT configured on the L3SW or router (filtered by ACL)
* (Req. 5) No VLAN has been configured on the ports targeted for DHRC operation on the L2SW for Host Network, CNI Network, and Application Data Network.

==== Parameter Design Prerequisites and Verification Method

The parameters used in this manual are managed in a separate parameter sheet.
Before starting work, verify that the parameters required to construct the DCI Management Cluster and other components have been entered,
and that there are no undetermined items.

Parameters specific to DCI Tenant Logical Infrastructure are not included in the scope of this manual.
Prepare them before performing Chapter 3, "Tenant Construction," of the Operations Manual.

The settings, command examples, and parameters in YAML files described in this manual are examples.
When performing the actual work, replace them with the values specified in the parameter sheet.

=== Documentation Policy and Procedure Description Rules

==== Procedure Documentation Policy

The procedures described in this document are written so that even a person performing construction work for the first time
can reproduce the work by following the described content.
Each procedure clearly states the purpose of the work, Execution Steps, and the method for verifying the Execution Result,
and describes Rollback Steps or the starting point for re-execution in case of failure.
Execution Steps include all commands to be entered and operations to be performed without omission,
with expected output examples and screen display examples included where necessary.

Variable elements such as IP addresses and version numbers are managed in the separate Parameter Sheet,
and procedures include both representative values and the corresponding variable names.
Representative values are provided to help understand the input format and value examples,
and must be replaced with the values defined in the Parameter Sheet during actual construction.
This approach minimizes the impact of changes when parameters are modified,
while also taking future procedure automation into consideration.

==== Procedure Description Rules

Each work procedure described in this document is, in principle, written based on the following structure.

* Purpose of the work
* Execution Steps
  ** Command Example
  ** Execution Result example
* Verification Steps after execution
  ** Verification Command Example
  ** Expected Verification Result
* Rollback Steps in case of failure

However, for tasks such as failure response or tasks that are only performed when necessary, it may not be possible to explicitly state all items due to their nature.

Also, depending on the work content, Verification Steps may be described collectively after multiple Execution Steps have been performed.
Rollback Steps may also describe the response for multiple procedures collectively.

==== Notes on Environment and Version Differences

The screen captures, Command Examples, Execution Results, and Verification Results described in this document are examples for illustrative purposes only.
Depending on the environment used, software version, and configuration state, some displayed content or wording may differ; however, this does not affect whether a procedure can be performed or whether the result is correct, as long as it does not significantly deviate from the described content.

Also, software version numbers and various parameter setting values described in this document are also examples; the values actually used are managed in the separate Parameter Sheet.

==== Notation Conventions

The following notation is used in subsequent chapters.

* `@k8s node`:
  **Work to be executed on the K8s Node of "DCI Management Cluster" is shown.** The K8s Node is the so-called Worker Node.
* `@control plane`:
  **Work to be executed on the K8s Control Plane of "DCI Management Cluster" is shown.**
  For the control plane of DCI Management Cluster, even in a redundant configuration, work performed on one control plane is sufficient. In this case, execute consistently on one of the control planes (e.g., Control Plane node1).

==== Usernames and Passwords

In this document, default usernames and passwords are used for OSS and other software.

== System Construction Overview

This chapter describes the overall configuration of the DCI System targeted for construction in this manual, and the positioning of the construction work.
The configuration and network configuration shown in this chapter are centered primarily on the "DCI Management Cluster" constructed in Chapter 3.
Chapter 4, on the other hand, covers the initial configuration of Physical Resources (COTS Servers, etc.) used for constructing DCI Tenant Logical Infrastructure.
Follow the construction workflow shown in Section 2.4, understand the overall picture in this chapter, then execute the procedures in Chapters 3 and 4 in order.

This section describes the system configuration overview of the DCI System assumed to be constructed throughout this manual.

[[network_configuration]]
=== Network Configuration
An example of the prerequisite network configuration for the work in this manual is shown below. +
The network configuration covered in this document is a minimum configuration example for constructing DCI Management Cluster and DCI Tenant Logical Infrastructure.
It is assumed that VLAN IDs and subnets for the number of tenants are designed and reserved in advance for tenant networks (host, CNI, application data).
The specific IP address ranges and VLAN IDs for each network are designed according to the environment and scale.
Note: Take care during IP address design to avoid overlap with subnets used internally by Kubernetes and CRI-O.


.Network Configuration
image::media/media/image01_en.png[width=1000]

The procedures shown aim to install and configure the Components listed below so that DCI Manager and DCI Physical Infrastructure are in an operable state as a DCI System.

The DCI Management Cluster is accessed from an operation terminal (DCI Operator).

If constructing the DCI Management Cluster in a virtual machine (VM) environment, refer to <<dci_management_cluster_vm_setup>> in the Appendix.

=== Construction Workflow

The construction work performed in this manual is broadly divided into the following two procedures.

* Procedure to construct DCI Management Cluster and deploy DCI Manager and DCI Physical Infrastructure
* Procedure to perform initial configuration of various resources used in DCI Tenant Logical Infrastructure

The overall construction workflow is shown in the following diagram. +
By executing these tasks from top to bottom, the DCI environment can be constructed step by step. +
The numbers in the diagram correspond to each chapter number. +
In subsequent chapters, specific work procedures are described in order for each work block shown in this diagram.

.Construction Workflow
image::media/media/flow0_en.png[width=432]

== DCI Management Cluster Construction Procedure

=== Initial Construction

This section describes the initial construction procedure required before starting DCI Management Cluster construction. +
Specifically, this section describes the PG-CDI initial construction procedure.

This section shows the procedure corresponding to the section enclosed in the red box in "2.4. Construction Workflow".

image::media/media/flow1_en.png[width=432]

.Rollback Steps
If a problem occurs in the procedure of each section in this chapter, in principle restart the procedure of that section from the beginning. +
Note: If a rollback method or re-execution starting point is explicitly stated within an individual procedure, follow that guidance.

==== PG-CDI Initial Construction

===== Setup

Refer to the following PG-CDI manual and perform PG-CDI setup.

 Reference: "PRIMERGY CDI V1.1 System Construction Procedure Manual"

Setup Overview
****
* Hardware installation
* Hardware setup
** Management server startup
** Client Linux-PC startup
** Director initial configuration
** PCIe switch initial configuration
* Software setup
** Guest OS initial configuration and startup
** CDI management software configuration
* Certificate configuration
****

===== Group Creation

Refer to the following PG-CDI manual and create resource group 0.

 Reference: "PRIMERGY CDI V1.1 System Operations Administrator Guide" Appendix A.1.3 Group Creation

===== Symbolic Link Configuration

On the client Linux-PC (ubuntu) started during PG-CDI setup, create a symbolic link from the path where CDI commands are installed as the ubuntu user (/home/ubuntu/bin) to the CDI control path (/home/cdiadmin/bin). +
Note: If the /home/cdiadmin directory does not exist, create it.

.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ sudo mkdir -p /home/cdiadmin
$ sudo ln -s /home/ubuntu/bin /home/cdiadmin/bin
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
None in particular
----

.Verification Steps
[source,console]
----
$ sudo su
# cd ../cdiadmin
# ls -l
# exit
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
total 0
lrwxrwxrwx 1 root root 16 Apr  7 20:03 bin -> /home/ubuntu/bin
----

===== expect Module Installation

Install the expect module on the client Linux-PC as an OSS required for PG-CDI control.

.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ sudo apt install expect
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
(omitted)
The following NEW packages will be installed:
  expect
(omitted)
----

.Verification Steps
[source,console]
----
$ sudo apt list expect
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
Listing... Done
expect/jammy,now 5.45.4-2build1 amd64 [installed]
----

===== CDI Management Software Account Creation

The existing cdiadmin account is for DCI Operator use; separately create an account to be used by physical infrastructure components from the client Linux-PC. +
The account name shall be "dhrcsystem".

.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
ubuntu@clientpc:~$ cdictl -u $URL auth login
Enter username: cdiadmin
Password for cdiadmin: (cdiadmin password)
Login successful.
cdi: cdictl auth user add dhrcsystem
Password for dhrcsystem: (any new password for dhrcsystem)
Confirm password for dhrcsystem: (any new password for dhrcsystem)
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
Success
----

.Verification Steps
[source,console]
----
cdi: cdictl auth user list
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
Success
[{'username': 'cdiadmin'}, {'username': 'dhrcsystem'}]
----

This completes "PG-CDI Initial Construction".

==== OS Installation and Initial Configuration

This section describes the installation procedure assuming Red Hat Enterprise Linux server 9.6 (RHEL9.6) is used as the OS to install. +
The target devices are all COTS Servers of the DCI Management Server in <<physical_server_config_components>>.

This section describes the installation method using the virtual console of the integrated Remote Management Controller (iRMC). +
Note: iRMC is a dedicated remote management controller included as standard in Fujitsu servers.

Use the DVD ISO image file as the RHEL installation ISO image.

.Prerequisites
* An iRMC user account (username, password) must be registered in advance.
* The DVD ISO image file for the installation target must be available. Example: rhel-9.6-x86++_++64-dvd.iso (rhel-<RHEL Version>-x86_64-dvd.iso)

===== RHEL Installation

. Log in to iRMC. +
+
--
Enter the username and password and press Submit.

image::media/media/image55_en.jpg[width=1000]
--

. Connect to iRMC and launch Video Redirection.
+
--
image::media/media/image3_en.png[width=1000]
--

+
--
Registration example: Select "rhel-9.6-x86++_++64-dvd.iso" (rhel-<RHEL Version>-x86_64-dvd.iso)

--

+

+
--
image::media/media/12_en.jpg[width=1000]

+
--
image::media/media/13_en.jpg[width=1000]

+
--
image::media/media/14_en.jpg[width=1000]
--

+
--
image::media/media/15_en.jpg[width=1000]
--
. When the login screen appears, installation is complete.
+
--
image::media/media/image21_en.png[width=1000]
--

. After logging in, execute "5.11.1. RHEL Subscription Application" described in the Appendix.

. (Only if a proxy is required for external communication) Edit /etc/dnf/dnf.conf.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ sudoedit /etc/dnf/dnf.conf
※ Edit as follows.
----

[source,bash]
----
http_proxy=http://[プロキシのIPアドレス]:[プロキシのポート番号]/
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
None in particular
----

.Verification Steps
[source,console]
----
$ more /etc/dnf/dnf.conf
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
http_proxy=http://[プロキシのIPアドレス]:[プロキシのポート番号]/
----
--

==== MAAS Server Construction

Execute this procedure on the MAAS server.
--
image::media/media/image_maincomponent_maas_en.png[width=1000]
--
For MAAS server construction, install snap, lxd, and maas in that order.

===== snap Installation

. Add the EPEL repository to make it usable.
+

----

.Verification Result

lrwxrwxrwx 1 root root 19  Apr 17 15:32 /snap -> /var/lib/snapd/snap/
----
--

. Create a shell script (snap.sh) that adds /snap/bin to PATH.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ cat << 'EOF' | sudo tee snap.sh
# shellcheck shell=sh
# Expand $PATH to include the directory where snappy applications go.
snap_bin_path="/snap/bin"
if [ -n "${PATH##*${snap_bin_path}}" ] && [ -n "${PATH##*${snap_bin_path}:*}" ]; then
    export PATH="$PATH:${snap_bin_path}"
fi
EOF
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
# shellcheck shell=sh
# Expand $PATH to include the directory where snappy applications go.
snap_bin_path="/snap/bin"
if [ -n "${PATH##*${snap_bin_path}}" ] && [ -n "${PATH##*${snap_bin_path}:*}" ]; then
    export PATH="$PATH:${snap_bin_path}"
fi
----

.Verification Steps
[source,console]
----
$ more snap.sh
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
# shellcheck shell=sh
# Expand $PATH to include the directory where snappy applications go.
snap_bin_path="/snap/bin"
if [ -n "${PATH##*${snap_bin_path}}" ] && [ -n "${PATH##*${snap_bin_path}:*}" ]; then
    export PATH="$PATH:${snap_bin_path}"
fi
----

$ sudo firewall-cmd --permanent --add-port=8443/udp

===== Manager NW Subnet DHCP Configuration

To execute OS deployment with MAAS, PXE boot is required; enable DHCP on the Manager NW Subnet for this purpose.

. First, log in to the MAAS server GUI. The Username and Password are those configured with maas createadmin in the previous section.

  URL:http://<MAAS IPAddr>:5240/MAAS/r
--
image::media/media/image57.jpg[width=1000]
--

. After logging in, the initial configuration screen appears; press "Skip (to user) setup" several times to complete the initial configuration.
+
--
image::media/media/image58.jpg[width=1000]
image::media/media/image59.jpg[width=1000]
image::media/media/image60.jpg[width=1000]
--

. First, enable DHCP. When icons appear on the left, click Subnet, then click the VLAN (untagged) for the Manager NW subnet (<MGR-Subnet>; "172.28.31.0/24" in the figure example).
+
--
image::media/media/image69.jpg[width=1000]
--

. Press "Configure DHCP".
+
--
image::media/media/image70.jpg[width=1000]
--

. Select the MAAS server for Rack controller and the Manager NW subnet (<MGR-Subnet>; "172.28.31.0/24" in the figure example) for Select subnet, then click Configure DHCP. +
  Verify that the Reserved dynamic range created at this point does not include the GW IP (e.g., 172.28.31.1). If it does, change the Reserved dynamic range.
+
--
image::media/media/image74.jpg[width=1000]
--

. After this, on the Subnet screen, the Manager NW DHCP changes from "No DHCP" to "MAAS-provided".
+
--
image::media/media/image75.jpg[width=1000]
--

. Next, configure DHCP. Click Subnet, then click the Manager NW subnet (<MGR-Subnet>; "172.28.31.0/24" in the figure example).
+
--
image::media/media/image61.jpg[width=1000]
--

. Press "Address reservation".
+
--
image::media/media/image62.jpg[width=1000]
--

. First, configure Static DHCP leases. This registers pairs of each server's Manager NW NIC MAC address and a fixed IP address to be always assigned. +
  Press the "Reserve static DHCP lease" button.
+
--
image::media/media/image63.jpg[width=1000]
--

. Register the IP address and MAC address pair (Comment is optional). Then press the "Reserve static DHCP lease" button.
+
--
image::media/media/image64.jpg[width=1000]
--

. This ensures that, for example, the server with MAC address aa.bb.cc.dd.ee.ff is always assigned IP address 172.28.31.31.
+
Repeat this operation for the Manager NW NIC MAC addresses of each tenant infrastructure server.
+
--
image::media/media/image65.jpg[width=1000]
--

. Next, configure Reserved ranges. This configures the "excluded" IP address pool range that MAAS automatically assigns to VM Manager NW NICs. +
Press Reserve range → Reserve range button.
+
--
image::media/media/image66.jpg[width=1000]
--

. Set the Start IP address and End IP address, then press Reserve. +
For example, to set the IP address pool range for VM Manager NW NICs to 172.28.31.41 through 172.28.31.49, configure the following under Reserve range. +
　①172.28.31.1～172.28.31.40 +
　②172.28.31.50～172.28.31.190 +
  ※ Ensure that the GW IP (e.g., 172.28.31.1) is included. +
  ※ There is no problem if IP addresses registered in "Static DHCP leases" are included within the "Reserve range".
+
--
image::media/media/image67.jpg[width=1000]
--

. VM Manager NW NICs are now assigned addresses in the range 172.28.31.41 through 172.28.31.49.
+
--
image::media/media/image68.jpg[width=1000]
--

===== RHEL Image Creation for MAAS Registration

. Use Packer to create an image for MAAS. (※ Example of creation on Ubuntu; this may be performed on a server completely unrelated to the DCI System)
** Packer must be installed
*** Prerequisite: Ubuntu version 22.04.5 or later
*** Reference: https://developer.hashicorp.com/packer/tutorials/docker-get-started/get-started-install-cli
** qemu-related packages must be installed
*** Example on Ubuntu: sudo apt install libnbd-bin nbdkit fuse2fs qemu-utils

. Create a RHEL 9.6 image for MAAS
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ git clone https://github.com/canonical/packer-maas.git
$ cd packer-maas/rhel9
$ vi rhel9.pkr.hcl
  packer {
    required_version = ">= 1.7.0"
    required_plugins {
      qemu = {
        version = "1.1.1"  ←★Change
     :
  source "qemu" "rhel9" {
    disk_size        =  "6G"  ←★Change

$ export ISO=/XXXX/rhel-9.6-x86_64-dvd.iso  ← Set the path to the rhel-9.6-x86_64-dvd.iso image file (rhel-<RHEL Version>-x86_64-dvd.iso)
$ sudo -E make
  : approx. 15 minutes
  :
rhel9.tar.gz is created; rename it to, for example, rhel9_6.tar.gz for use.
----
--

===== RHEL Image Registration to MAAS
. Store the created RHEL image for MAAS (rhel9_6.tar.gz) on the MAAS server.

. Log in to the MAAS server and execute the following in the directory containing the RHEL image. +
※ Record the API key displayed in step ① below because it will be registered in the `t_maas` table of the Configuration Management DB.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ sudo /snap/bin/maas apikey --username admin … ①​
$ maas login admin http://localhost:5240/MAAS/ <API key displayed in step ①>
$ maas admin boot-resources create name='rhel/9.6-custom' title='RHEL 9.6 Custom' architecture='amd64/generic' filetype='tgz' content@=rhel9_6.tar.gz
----
--

. MAAS GUI image after registration
+
--
image::media/media/image54.png[width=1000]
--

===== MAAS Server Construction Rollback Steps

If uninstalling MAAS due to defects or other issues, execute the following. +
If reinstalling, re-execute from section 3.2.2.3.

[source,console]
.Execution Steps
----
$ sudo snap remove maas maas-test-db
----

=== DCI Management Cluster Construction

This section describes the procedure to construct a Kubernetes cluster for use as the DCI Management Cluster. +
Specifically, this section describes the Kubernetes and CRI-O installation and configuration, pre-construction preparation required for cluster construction, and Kubernetes cluster construction procedures.

This section shows the procedure corresponding to the section enclosed in the red box in "2.4. Construction Workflow".

image::media/media/flow3_en.png[width=432]

Also, the target servers for construction correspond to the section enclosed in the red box in the Physical Server configuration of the construction target scope shown below. (Control Plane x3, K8s Node x2) +
Note: Procedures up to section 3.3.3 may be performed on multiple servers simultaneously.
--
image::media/media/image_maincomponent_k8s_en.png[width=1000]
--

.Rollback Steps
If a problem occurs in the procedure of each section in this chapter, in principle redo that procedure. +
Note: If a rollback method or re-execution starting point is explicitly stated within an individual procedure, follow that guidance.

==== K8s Installation

. Since Calico is used as the CNI, back up the CRI-O bridge.

$ cd ~/
$ sudo rm -rf /etc/cni/net.d/

$ sudo mkdir -p /etc/systemd/system/crio.service.d
$ sudoedit /etc/systemd/system/crio.service.d/00-proxy.conf

[source,bash]

[Service]
Environment="HTTP_PROXY=http://[プロキシのIPアドレス]:[プロキシのポート番号]"
Environment="HTTPS_PROXY=http://[プロキシのIPアドレス]:[プロキシのポート番号]"
Environment="NO_PROXY=localhost,127.0.0.1,[Manager NW CIDR],[Host NW CIDR],[CNI NW CIDR],[Pod CIDR],[Service CIDR],.cluster.local"
----

. Edit calico.yaml. Specifically, search for "autodetect" and add the following content immediately after it.
+

.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ vi calico.yaml
  # Auto-detect the BGP IP address.
  - name: IP
    value: "autodetect"
  # Set Auto-Detection Method of Network Interface ※ Add the following; this line is a comment and does not need to be added.
  - name: IP_AUTODETECTION_METHOD
    value: kubernetes-internal-ip
----
Verify that the content is correct before saving.

==== K8s Cluster Construction
The content of this section assumes an environment with 3 Kubernetes Control Planes in a redundant configuration.

===== Work on the 1st Control Plane

. Create kube-vip.yaml and place it in /etc/kubernetes/manifests/.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ vi kube-vip.yaml (configuration content as follows)
$ sudo mv kube-vip.yaml /etc/kubernetes/manifests/
----
[source,bash]
----
apiVersion: v1
kind: Pod
metadata:
  name: kube-vip
  namespace: kube-system
spec:
  containers:
  - args:
    - manager
    env:
    - name: vip_arp
      value: "true"
    - name: port
      value: "6443"
    - name: vip_interface
      value: eno0            # ★Specify the Manager NW interface according to your environment
    - name: vip_cidr
      value: "24"
    - name: cp_enable
      value: "true"
    - name: cp_namespace
      value: kube-system
    - name: vip_ddns
      value: "false"
    - name: svc_enable
      value: "true"
    - name: vip_leaderelection
      value: "true"
    - name: vip_leaseduration
      value: "5"
    - name: vip_renewdeadline
      value: "3"
    - name: vip_retryperiod
      value: "1"
    - name: address
      value: "172.28.193.30"  # ★Specify an unused virtual VIP address (<MGR-IPAddr-VIP>)
    image: ghcr.io/kube-vip/kube-vip:v0.8.4
    imagePullPolicy: Always
    name: kube-vip
    resources: {}
    securityContext:
      capabilities:
        add:
        - NET_ADMIN
        - NET_RAW
    volumeMounts:
    - mountPath: /etc/kubernetes/admin.conf
      name: kubeconfig
  hostAliases:
  - hostnames:
    - kubernetes
    ip: 127.0.0.1
  hostNetwork: true
  volumes:
  - hostPath:
      path: /etc/kubernetes/super-admin.conf  # ★Remove "super-" for the 2nd and subsequent nodes
    name: kubeconfig
----
Verify that the content is correct before saving.

apiVersion: kubeadm.k8s.io/v1beta4
kind: InitConfiguration
localAPIEndpoint:
  advertiseAddress: "172.28.193.10"  # ★Manager NW interface address (<MGR-IPAddr-CP1>)
  bindPort: 6443
nodeRegistration:
  criSocket: unix:///var/run/crio/crio.sock
  imagePullPolicy: IfNotPresent
  imagePullSerial: true
  name: "d1-f01-k8s-master01"        # ★Hostname (<mgr-master01>)
  taints: null
---
apiVersion: kubeadm.k8s.io/v1beta4
kind: ClusterConfiguration
kubernetesVersion: "v1.36.3"
controlPlaneEndpoint: "172.28.193.30:6443"  # ★Virtual VIP address :6443(<MGR-IPAddr-VIP>:6443)
networking:
  podSubnet: "10.244.0.0/16"    # ★Modify according to your environment (determined by the CNI used)
  serviceSubnet: "10.96.0.0/12"  # ★Modify according to your environment (usually keep as-is)
imageRepository: registry.k8s.io
clusterName: kubernetes
certificatesDir: /etc/kubernetes/pki
etcd:
  local:
    dataDir: /var/lib/etcd
apiServer: {}
controllerManager: {}
scheduler: {}
dns: {}
proxy: {}
caCertificateValidityPeriod: 87600h0m0s
certificateValidityPeriod: 8760h0m0s
encryptionAlgorithm: RSA-2048

. Execute join. +

+

.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ sudo kubeadm join 172.28.193.30:6443 --token xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx \
        --discovery-token-ca-cert-hash sha256:yyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyy \
        --control-plane --certificate-key zzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzz \
        --cri-socket=unix:///var/run/crio/crio.sock
($ sudo kubeadm join <MGR-IPAddr-VIP>:6443 --token xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx \
         --discovery-token-ca-cert-hash sha256:yyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyyy \
         --control-plane --certificate-key zzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzz \
         --cri-socket=unix:///var/run/crio/crio.sock)
----

Run 'kubectl get nodes' to see this node join the cluster.

. Note: Occasionally, joining the 2nd or 3rd control plane may fail. In that case, delete the configuration using the following procedure and redo the work from "Work on the 2nd and 3rd Control Planes".
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ sudo kubeadm reset
$ sudo rm -rf /etc/kubernetes/manifests/kube-apiserver.yaml
$ sudo rm -rf /etc/kubernetes/manifests/kube-controller-manager.yaml
$ sudo rm -rf /etc/kubernetes/manifests/kube-scheduler.yaml
$ sudo rm -rf /etc/kubernetes/manifests/etcd.yaml
$ sudo rm -rf /var/lib/etcd
$ sudo reboot
----
--
+
If join still fails after retrying the procedure above, perform the following recovery procedure and retry join.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
# 1. Install the etcdctl command @1st control plane
$ ETCD_VER=v3.7.1
$ wget https://github.com/etcd-io/etcd/releases/download/${ETCD_VER}/etcd-${ETCD_VER}-linux-amd64.tar.gz
$ tar xzf etcd-${ETCD_VER}-linux-amd64.tar.gz
$ sudo cp etcd-${ETCD_VER}-linux-amd64/etcdctl /usr/local/bin/

# 2. Check the etcd member list @1st control plane
$ sudo ETCDCTL_API=3 /usr/local/bin/etcdctl \
  --endpoints=https://127.0.0.1:2379 \
  --cacert=/etc/kubernetes/pki/etcd/ca.crt \
  --cert=/etc/kubernetes/pki/etcd/healthcheck-client.crt \
  --key=/etc/kubernetes/pki/etcd/healthcheck-client.key \
  member list

# 3. If the member for the control plane that failed to join remains in etcd, delete that member @1st control plane
$ sudo ETCDCTL_API=3 /usr/local/bin/etcdctl \
  --endpoints=https://127.0.0.1:2379 \
  --cacert=/etc/kubernetes/pki/etcd/ca.crt \
  --cert=/etc/kubernetes/pki/etcd/healthcheck-client.crt \
  --key=/etc/kubernetes/pki/etcd/healthcheck-client.key \
  member remove <memberID>

# 4. Reset the node @control plane that failed to join
$ sudo kubeadm reset -f
$ sudo rm -rf /etc/kubernetes
$ sudo rm -rf /var/lib/etcd

# 5. Check for a token to use for join @1st control plane
$ sudo kubeadm token list

# 6. If no usable token exists above, issue a new join token @1st control plane
$ sudo kubeadm token create --print-join-command

# 7. Check for a certificate-key (kubeadm-certs) to use for join @1st control plane
$ kubectl get secret -n kube-system kubeadm-certs

# 8. If no usable certificate-key exists above, regenerate the certificate-key @1st control plane
$ sudo kubeadm init phase upload-certs --upload-certs
----

--

=== Pre-Setup for DCI Manager and DCI Physical Infrastructure Deployment

This section describes the pre-setup procedure for deploying DCI Manager and DCI Physical Infrastructure. +
Specifically, this section describes the installation of helm, git, buildah, go, yq, and NTP server configuration.

This section shows the procedure corresponding to the section enclosed in the red box in "2.4. Construction Workflow".

image::media/media/flow4_en.png[width=432]

==== Firewall Configuration `@control plane※`, `@k8s node`
There are sections to execute on all control planes used for DCI Management Cluster and sections to execute on all K8s Nodes.

NOTE: To avoid interfering with NAT processing inside K8s, restrict access to K8s Service NodePorts on network equipment (FW) outside K8s, rather than with firewalld or iptables.

===== Configuration on All Control Planes of DCI Management Cluster

. Create a firewall configuration script.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ vi firewall_manager-controlplane.sh
※ Create as follows.
----

[source,bash]
----
#!/usr/bin/env bash
PODS_CIDR="Pod CIDR setting" #example: a.b.c.d/16
MANAGER_CIDR="Manager CIDR setting" #example: e.f.g.h/24
DCI_OPERATOR_CIDR="DCI Operator terminal CIDR setting" #example: i.j.k.l/24
BASTION_IP="IP address of the bastion host used for Harbor and Grafana GUI access" #example: m.n.o.p
LB_VIP="VIP for LoadBalancer type Service" #example: q.r.s.t
# zone
firewall-cmd --permanent --new-zone=k8s-node
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-node --add-forward
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-node --add-source=${MANAGER_CIDR}
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-node --add-source=${DCI_OPERATOR_CIDR}
firewall-cmd --permanent --new-zone=k8s-pod
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-pod --add-forward
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-pod --add-source=${PODS_CIDR}
# ssh
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-node --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${MANAGER_CIDR} port port=22 protocol=tcp accept"
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-node --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${DCI_OPERATOR_CIDR} port port=22 protocol=tcp accept"
# kube-apiserver
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-node --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${MANAGER_CIDR} port port=6443 protocol=tcp accept"
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-pod --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${PODS_CIDR} port port=6443 protocol=tcp accept"
# kubelet API
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-node --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${MANAGER_CIDR} port port=10250 protocol=tcp accept"
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-pod --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${PODS_CIDR} port port=10250 protocol=tcp accept"
# kube-proxy
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-node --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${MANAGER_CIDR} port port=10256 protocol=tcp accept"
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-pod --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${PODS_CIDR} port port=10256 protocol=tcp accept"
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-node --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${MANAGER_CIDR} port port=10249 protocol=tcp accept"
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-pod --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${PODS_CIDR} port port=10249 protocol=tcp accept"
# kube-controller-manager
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-node --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${MANAGER_CIDR} port port=10257 protocol=tcp accept"
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-pod --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${PODS_CIDR} port port=10257 protocol=tcp accept"
# kube-scheduler
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-node --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${MANAGER_CIDR} port port=10259 protocol=tcp accept"
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-pod --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${PODS_CIDR} port port=10259 protocol=tcp accept"
# etcd
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-node --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${MANAGER_CIDR} port port=2379 protocol=tcp accept"
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-pod --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${PODS_CIDR} port port=2379 protocol=tcp accept"
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-node --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${MANAGER_CIDR} port port=2380 protocol=tcp accept"
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-pod --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${PODS_CIDR} port port=2380 protocol=tcp accept"
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-node --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${MANAGER_CIDR} port port=2381 protocol=tcp accept"
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-pod --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${PODS_CIDR} port port=2381 protocol=tcp accept"
# calico BGP
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-node --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${MANAGER_CIDR} port port=179 protocol=tcp accept"
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-pod --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${PODS_CIDR} port port=179 protocol=tcp accept"
# calico IPIP
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-node --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${MANAGER_CIDR} protocol value=4 accept"
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-pod --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${PODS_CIDR} protocol value=4 accept"
# calico Typha
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-node --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${MANAGER_CIDR} port port=5473 protocol=tcp accept"
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-pod --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${PODS_CIDR} port port=5473 protocol=tcp accept"
# node-exporter
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-node --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${MANAGER_CIDR} port port=9100 protocol=tcp accept"
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-pod --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${PODS_CIDR} port port=9100 protocol=tcp accept"
# vector (for receiving rsyslog)
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-node --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${MANAGER_CIDR} port port=514 protocol=tcp accept"
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-pod --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${PODS_CIDR} port port=514 protocol=tcp accept"
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-node --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${MANAGER_CIDR} port port=514 protocol=udp accept"
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-pod --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${PODS_CIDR} port port=514 protocol=udp accept"

# LoadBalancer type Service
firewall-cmd --permanent --direct --add-rule ipv4 raw PREROUTING 0 \
  -p tcp -s ${MANAGER_CIDR} -d ${LB_VIP} \
  -m multiport --dports 8443 -j ACCEPT
firewall-cmd --permanent --direct --add-rule ipv4 raw PREROUTING 0 \
  -p tcp -s ${PODS_CIDR} -d ${LB_VIP} \
  -m multiport --dports 8443 -j ACCEPT
firewall-cmd --permanent --direct --add-rule ipv4 raw PREROUTING 0 \
  -p tcp -s ${DCI_OPERATOR_CIDR} -d ${LB_VIP} \
  -m multiport --dports 8443 -j ACCEPT
firewall-cmd --permanent --direct --add-rule ipv4 raw PREROUTING 10 \
  -p tcp -d ${LB_VIP} -j DROP

# pod forward egress
firewall-cmd --permanent --new-policy pod-fwd-egress
firewall-cmd --permanent --policy pod-fwd-egress --set-target=ACCEPT
firewall-cmd --permanent --policy pod-fwd-egress --add-ingress-zone=k8s-pod
firewall-cmd --permanent --policy pod-fwd-egress --add-egress-zone=ANY

firewall-cmd --permanent --new-policy k8s-node-to-pod
firewall-cmd --permanent --policy k8s-node-to-pod --set-target=ACCEPT
firewall-cmd --permanent --policy k8s-node-to-pod --add-ingress-zone=k8s-node
firewall-cmd --permanent --policy k8s-node-to-pod --add-egress-zone=k8s-pod

firewall-cmd --permanent --new-policy k8s-pod-to-node
firewall-cmd --permanent --policy k8s-pod-to-node --set-target=ACCEPT
firewall-cmd --permanent --policy k8s-pod-to-node --add-ingress-zone=k8s-pod
firewall-cmd --permanent --policy k8s-pod-to-node --add-egress-zone=k8s-node

firewall-cmd --permanent --new-policy k8s-pod-to-pod
firewall-cmd --permanent --policy k8s-pod-to-pod --set-target=ACCEPT
firewall-cmd --permanent --policy k8s-pod-to-pod --add-ingress-zone=k8s-pod
firewall-cmd --permanent --policy k8s-pod-to-pod --add-egress-zone=k8s-pod

firewall-cmd --permanent --new-policy k8s-node-to-node
firewall-cmd --permanent --policy k8s-node-to-node --set-target=ACCEPT
firewall-cmd --permanent --policy k8s-node-to-node --add-ingress-zone=k8s-node
firewall-cmd --permanent --policy k8s-node-to-node --add-egress-zone=k8s-node

# public disabled
for i in interfaces sources services ports protocols; do
  for v in $(firewall-cmd --zone=public --list-$i); do
    firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=public --remove-${i/%?/}="$v"
  done
done
# reload
firewall-cmd --reload

[source,bash]

#!/usr/bin/env bash
PODS_CIDR="Pod CIDR setting"
MANAGER_CIDR="Manager CIDR setting"
DCI_OPERATOR_CIDR="DCI Operator terminal CIDR setting"
BASTION_IP="IP address of the bastion host used for Harbor and Grafana GUI access"
# zone
firewall-cmd --permanent --new-zone=k8s-node
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-node --add-forward
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-node --add-source=${MANAGER_CIDR}
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-node --add-source=${DCI_OPERATOR_CIDR}
firewall-cmd --permanent --new-zone=k8s-pod
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-pod --add-forward
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-pod --add-source=${PODS_CIDR}
# ssh
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-node --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${MANAGER_CIDR} port port=22 protocol=tcp accept"
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-node --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${DCI_OPERATOR_CIDR} port port=22 protocol=tcp accept"
# kubelet API
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-node --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${MANAGER_CIDR} port port=10250 protocol=tcp accept"
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-pod --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${PODS_CIDR} port port=10250 protocol=tcp accept"
# kube-proxy
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-node --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${MANAGER_CIDR} port port=10256 protocol=tcp accept"
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-pod --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${PODS_CIDR} port port=10256 protocol=tcp accept"
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-node --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${MANAGER_CIDR} port port=10249 protocol=tcp accept"
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-pod --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${PODS_CIDR} port port=10249 protocol=tcp accept"
# node-exporter
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-node --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${MANAGER_CIDR} port port=9100 protocol=tcp accept"
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-pod --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${PODS_CIDR} port port=9100 protocol=tcp accept"
# calico IPIP
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-node --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${MANAGER_CIDR} protocol value=4 accept"
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-pod --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${PODS_CIDR} protocol value=4 accept"
# calico Typha
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-node --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${MANAGER_CIDR} port port=5473 protocol=tcp accept"
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-pod --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${PODS_CIDR} port port=5473 protocol=tcp accept"
# calico BGP
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-node --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${MANAGER_CIDR} port port=179 protocol=tcp accept"
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-pod --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${PODS_CIDR} port port=179 protocol=tcp accept"
# vector (for receiving rsyslog)
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-node --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${MANAGER_CIDR} port port=514 protocol=tcp accept"
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-pod --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${PODS_CIDR} port port=514 protocol=tcp accept"
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-node --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${MANAGER_CIDR} port port=514 protocol=udp accept"
firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=k8s-pod --add-rich-rule="rule family=ipv4 source address=${PODS_CIDR} port port=514 protocol=udp accept"

# pod forward egress
firewall-cmd --permanent --new-policy pod-fwd-egress
firewall-cmd --permanent --policy pod-fwd-egress --set-target=ACCEPT
firewall-cmd --permanent --policy pod-fwd-egress --add-ingress-zone=k8s-pod
firewall-cmd --permanent --policy pod-fwd-egress --add-egress-zone=ANY

firewall-cmd --permanent --new-policy k8s-node-to-pod
firewall-cmd --permanent --policy k8s-node-to-pod --set-target=ACCEPT
firewall-cmd --permanent --policy k8s-node-to-pod --add-ingress-zone=k8s-node
firewall-cmd --permanent --policy k8s-node-to-pod --add-egress-zone=k8s-pod

firewall-cmd --permanent --new-policy k8s-pod-to-node
firewall-cmd --permanent --policy k8s-pod-to-node --set-target=ACCEPT
firewall-cmd --permanent --policy k8s-pod-to-node --add-ingress-zone=k8s-pod
firewall-cmd --permanent --policy k8s-pod-to-node --add-egress-zone=k8s-node

firewall-cmd --permanent --new-policy k8s-pod-to-pod
firewall-cmd --permanent --policy k8s-pod-to-pod --set-target=ACCEPT
firewall-cmd --permanent --policy k8s-pod-to-pod --add-ingress-zone=k8s-pod
firewall-cmd --permanent --policy k8s-pod-to-pod --add-egress-zone=k8s-pod

firewall-cmd --permanent --new-policy k8s-node-to-node
firewall-cmd --permanent --policy k8s-node-to-node --set-target=ACCEPT
firewall-cmd --permanent --policy k8s-node-to-node --add-ingress-zone=k8s-node
firewall-cmd --permanent --policy k8s-node-to-node --add-egress-zone=k8s-node

# public disabled
for i in interfaces sources services ports protocols; do
  for v in $(firewall-cmd --zone=public --list-$i); do
    firewall-cmd --permanent --zone=public --remove-${i/%?/}="$v"
  done
done
# reload
firewall-cmd --reload

PODS_CIDR="Pod CIDR setting" #example: a.b.c.d/16
MANAGER_CIDR="Manager CIDR setting" #example: e.f.g.h/24
DCI_OPERATOR_CIDR="DCI Operator terminal CIDR setting" #example: i.j.k.l/24
BASTION_IP="IP address of the bastion host used for Harbor and Grafana GUI access" #example: m.n.o.p

==== Proxy Configuration (Only if a proxy is required for external communication) `@control plane※`, `@k8s node`
Execute on all control planes and all K8s Nodes used for DCI Management Cluster.

※ MITM proxies are not supported.

. Edit /etc/environment.

https_proxy=http://[プロキシのIPアドレス]:[プロキシのポート番号]/
no_proxy=localhost,127.0.0.1,[Manager NW CIDR],[Host NW CIDR],[CNI NW CIDR],[Pod CIDR],[Service CIDR],.cluster.local,.svc,kubernetes.default.svc,harbor-core,harbor-database,harbor-jobservice,harbor-nginx,harbor-portal,harbor-redis,harbor-registry,harbor-trivy,[Manager Harbor IP address]

=== DCI Manager Deployment

This section describes the procedure to deploy DCI Manager-related components that manage the entire DCI.

This section shows the procedure corresponding to the section enclosed in the red box in "2.4. Construction Workflow".

image::media/media/flow5_en.png[width=432]

※ In this section, if a procedure fails, restart that procedure from the beginning.

==== Environment Variable Configuration to Specify Material Versions `@control plane`, `@k8s node`

. Configure environment variables to specify material versions.
+
--
.Execution Steps
※ If the wrong TAG version is specified, delete it from ~/.bashrc and execute again.
[source,console]
----
$ echo "export TAG_MANAGER=1.0.0" >> ~/.bashrc (echo "export TAG_MANAGER=<Tag_Manager>" >> ~/.bashrc)
$ echo "export TAG_PHYSICAL=1.0.0" >> ~/.bashrc (echo "export TAG_PHYSICAL=<Tag_Physical>" >> ~/.bashrc)
$ echo "export TAG_TENANT=1.0.0" >> ~/.bashrc (echo "export TAG_TENANT=<Tag_Tenant>" >> ~/.bashrc)
$ echo "export TAG_COMMON=1.0.0" >> ~/.bashrc (echo "export TAG_COMMON=<Tag_Common>" >> ~/.bashrc)
$ source ~/.bashrc
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
None in particular
----

.Verification Steps
[source,console]
----
$ env | grep TAG
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
TAG_TENANT=1.0.0
TAG_MANAGER=1.0.0
TAG_PHYSICAL=1.0.0
TAG_COMMON=1.0.0
----
--

==== etcd Secret Encryption `@control plane`※

※ Item 1 below "Encryption configuration file (encryption-config.yaml)" is executed on the main control plane only, but the created /etc/kubernetes/encryption-config.yaml must be copied to the same directory on the other two control planes. +
   Also, all other items must be executed on all control planes.

. Create an "Encryption configuration file (encryption-config.yaml)" to prevent Kubernetes Secrets from being stored in plaintext on etcd.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ echo '{"apiVersion":"apiserver.config.k8s.io/v1","kind":"EncryptionConfiguration","resources":[{"resources":["secrets"],"providers":[{"aescbc":{"keys":[{"name":"key1"}]}},{"identity":{}}]}]}' | sudo --preserve-env=PATH sh -c 'yq e -P > /etc/kubernetes/encryption-config.yaml'
$ head -c 32 /dev/urandom | base64 | tr -d '\n' | sudo --preserve-env=PATH sh -c 'xargs -I {} yq -i e ".resources[0].providers[0].aescbc.keys[0].secret = \"{}\"" /etc/kubernetes/encryption-config.yaml'
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
None in particular
----

.Verification Steps
[source,console]
----
$ more /etc/kubernetes/encryption-config.yaml
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
※ The secret value is randomly generated.
----
apiVersion: apiserver.config.k8s.io/v1
kind: EncryptionConfiguration
resources:
  - resources:
      - secrets
    providers:
      - aescbc:
          keys:
            - name: key1
              secret: ********************************************
      - identity: {}
----
--

. Mount the created encryption-config.yaml to the kube-apiserver container and enable Secret encryption with those settings.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ sudo --preserve-env=PATH sh -c "yq -i '.spec.volumes += [{\"hostPath\":{\"path\":\"/etc/kubernetes/encryption-config.yaml\", \"type\":\"File\"}, \"name\":\"encryption-config\"}]' /etc/kubernetes/manifests/kube-apiserver.yaml"
$ sudo --preserve-env=PATH sh -c "yq -i '.spec.containers[0].volumeMounts += [{\"mountPath\":\"/etc/kubernetes/encryption-config.yaml\", \"name\":\"encryption-config\", \"readOnly\":true}]' /etc/kubernetes/manifests/kube-apiserver.yaml"
$ sudo --preserve-env=PATH sh -c "yq -i '.spec.containers[0].command += [\"--encryption-provider-config=/etc/kubernetes/encryption-config.yaml\"]' /etc/kubernetes/manifests/kube-apiserver.yaml"
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
None in particular
----

.Verification Steps
[source,console]
----
$ kubectl get pod -n kube-system kube-apiserver-<hostname> -o yaml
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
※ Verify that the following exists in the Verification Result yaml.
※ After Execution Steps, perform Verification Steps; it may take some time for results to be reflected after verification.
----
①
spec:
  containers:
  - command:
    - --encryption-provider-config=/etc/kubernetes/encryption-config.yaml
②
spec:
  containers:
  - command:
    volumeMounts:
    - mountPath: /etc/kubernetes/encryption-config.yaml
      name: encryption-config
      readOnly: true
③
spec:
  volumes:
  - hostPath:
      path: /etc/kubernetes/encryption-config.yaml
      type: File
    name: encryption-config
----

[source,console]

$ cd ~
$ git clone -b v${TAG_MANAGER} https://github.com/openkasugai/dci_manager

[source,console]
----
/etc/kubernetes/audit_policy.yaml
----
--

. Configure kube-apiserver.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ sudoedit /etc/kubernetes/manifests/kube-apiserver.yaml
① Add the following to spec.containers.command
---------------------------------------------------------------
        - --audit-policy-file=/etc/kubernetes/audit_policy.yaml
        - --audit-log-path=/var/log/kubernetes/audit/audit.log
        - --audit-log-maxsize=20
        - --audit-log-maxbackup=3
        - --audit-log-maxage=3
---------------------------------------------------------------
② Add the following to spec.containers.volumeMounts
---------------------------------------------------------------
        - mountPath: /etc/kubernetes/audit_policy.yaml
          name: audit
          readOnly: true
        - mountPath: /var/log/kubernetes/audit/
          name: audit-log
          readOnly: false
---------------------------------------------------------------
③ Add the following to spec.volumes
---------------------------------------------------------------
    - hostPath:
        path: /etc/kubernetes/audit_policy.yaml
        type: File
      name: audit
    - hostPath:
        path: /var/log/kubernetes/audit/
        type: DirectoryOrCreate
      name: audit-log
---------------------------------------------------------------
----

[source,console]
----
(After saving, kube-apiserver will be restarted)

----
None in particular

==== Kyverno Deployment and Proxy Configuration Policy Creation/Deployment (Only if a proxy is required for external communication) `@control plane`

. Register the Kyverno repository.

--

$ helm repo add kyverno https://kyverno.github.io/kyverno/

.Execution Steps

$ sudo dnf -y install nfs-utils-2.5.4-34.el9.x86_64
($ sudo dnf -y install nfs-utils-<NFS-utils Version>.x86_64)

[source,console]

NAME                            URL
nfs-subdir-external-provisioner https://kubernetes-sigs.github.io/nfs-subdir-external-provisioner/
----
--

. Edit values.yaml. `@control plane`
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ cd others/nfs-subdir-external-provisioner
$ vi values.yaml ※Modify the IP address and path below to match the NFS server used by DCI Manager.
----

[source,bash]
----
nfs:
  server: "<NFS server IP address>"
  path: "<NFS server export directory path>" ※
storageClass:
  name: "nfs-client"
  # The following are default values (uncomment to change)
  # defaultClass: false
  # allowVolumeExpansion: true
  # reclaimPolicy: "Delete"  # or "Retain"
  # archiveOnDelete: true

※ If sharing an NFS server across multiple environments, it is recommended to separate directory paths per environment and per DCI Management Cluster/tenant.
  For example, if the created export directory is /export/nfs, additionally create subdirectories per management cluster/tenant, and set those paths in <NFS server export directory path>.
  Example:
    DCI Management Cluster for Environment A (DCI Manager)
      · sudo mkdir /export/nfs/enva-mng
      · Set /export/nfs/enva-mng in <NFS server export directory path>
----
Verify that the content is correct before saving.

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
None in particular
----

[source,console]
----

. Edit gateway-lb.yaml.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ vi gateway-lb.yaml
※ Set 172.28.193.31 in the two locations in the file to a VIP for a LoadBalancer-type Service. Note that this is not the control plane VIP configured in the kube-vip manifest. Specify an available IP address in the Manager NW subnet.
----

[source,bash]
----
apiVersion: v1
kind: Service
metadata:
  name: gateway-lb
  namespace: nginx-gateway
  annotations:
    kube-vip.io/loadbalancerIPs: "172.28.193.31" # <LB-Service-VIP>
spec:
  type: LoadBalancer
  loadBalancerIP: 172.28.193.31 # <LB-Service-VIP>
  selector:
    app.kubernetes.io/name: gateway-nginx
  ports:
    - name: http
      port: 80
      targetPort: 80
      protocol: TCP
    - name: https
      port: 8443
      targetPort: 8443
      protocol: TCP
----

※ Set 172.28.193.31 in the file to a VIP for a LoadBalancer-type Service. Note that this is not the control plane VIP configured in the kube-vip manifest. Specify an available IP address in the Manager NW subnet.
----

[source,bash]
----
apiVersion: cert-manager.io/v1
kind: Certificate
metadata:
  name: vip-cert
  namespace: nginx-gateway
spec:
  secretName: vip-tls
  issuerRef:
    name: gateway-ca-issuer
    kind: Issuer
  dnsNames:
    - loki.local
    - db-api.local
  ipAddresses:
    - 172.28.193.31 # <LB-Service-VIP>
----

expose:
  type: nodePort
  tls:
    enabled: true
    certSource: auto
    auto:
      commonName: 172.28.193.30 #<MGR-IPAddr-VIP> *Modify configuration values according to the environment
  nodePort:
    name: harbor
    ports:
      http:
        port: 80
        nodePort: 30002
      https:
        port: 443
        nodePort: 30003
externalURL: https://172.28.193.30:30003 #<MGR-IPAddr-VIP> IP *Modify configuration values according to the environment
persistence:
  enabled: true
  persistentVolumeClaim:
    registry:
      storageClass: nfs-client
      accessMode: ReadWriteOnce
    jobservice:
      jobLog:
        storageClass: nfs-client
        accessMode: ReadWriteOnce
    database:
      storageClass: nfs-client
      accessMode: ReadWriteOnce
    redis:
      storageClass: nfs-client
      accessMode: ReadWriteOnce
    trivy:

      accessMode: ReadWriteOnce

nginx:

  tolerations:

            --set database.internal.image.repository=docker.io/goharbor/harbor-db \
            --set redis.internal.image.repository=docker.io/goharbor/redis-photon
(helm upgrade --install harbor harbor/harbor \

==== Storing Materials to Harbor
Refer to <<sec_component_list>> for the materials to store.

. Perform pre-preparation for material storage.

.. Configure environment variables. `@all nodes (control plane and k8s node)`
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ cd ~
$ echo "export HARBOR_MNG=<harbor-ip>" >> ~/.bashrc ※<harbor-ip>: IP address of the Manager-side Harbor (kube-vip IP address of the cluster where Harbor Pod is deployed in DCI Management Cluster)
$ source ~/.bashrc
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
None in particular
----

.Verification Steps
[source,console]
----
$ env | grep HARBOR
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
(omitted)
HARBOR_MNG=<harbor-ip>
----
--

.. Authenticate the certificate. `@all nodes (control plane and k8s node)`
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ kubectl get secrets -n harbor harbor-nginx -o jsonpath='{.data.ca\.crt}' | base64 -d > harbor_mng.crt
$ sudo cp harbor_mng.crt /etc/ssl/certs/
$ sudo update-ca-trust
$ sudo systemctl restart crio
$ sudo cp harbor_mng.crt /etc/pki/ca-trust/source/anchors/
$ sudo update-ca-trust extract
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
None in particular
----

.Verification Steps
[source, console]
----

.. Log in to Harbor and create a project. `@control plane`
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ sudo buildah login -u **username** -p **password** $HARBOR_MNG:30003
$ curl -X POST https://$HARBOR_MNG:30003/api/v2.0/projects -H "accept: application/json" -H "Content-Type: application/json" -u "**username**:**password**" -d '{ "project_name": "dci_manager", "public": true, "metadata": {"auto_scan": "true"}}'
$ curl -X POST https://$HARBOR_MNG:30003/api/v2.0/projects -H "accept: application/json" -H "Content-Type: application/json" -u "**username**:**password**" -d '{ "project_name": "dci_physical_infrastructure", "public": true, "metadata": {"auto_scan": "true"}}'
$ curl -X POST https://$HARBOR_MNG:30003/api/v2.0/projects -H "accept: application/json" -H "Content-Type: application/json" -u "**username**:**password**" -d '{ "project_name": "dci_tenant_infrastructure", "public": true, "metadata": {"auto_scan": "true"}}'
----

.. Build Resource Pool Manager materials into two types — "container image" and "Helm chart" — and register them to Harbor.

.Execution Steps
[source,console]

$ cd ~/dci_manager/resource-pool-manager/
$ vi Dockerfile #If a proxy is required for external communication, add proxy settings (refer to "6. Proxy settings content for Dockerfile")
$ vi Dockerfile.alert-receiver #If a proxy is required for external communication, add proxy settings (refer to "6. Proxy settings content for Dockerfile")
$ sudo make buildah-build IMG=$HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_manager/resource-pool-manager:${TAG_MANAGER} ALERT_RECEIVER_IMG=$HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_manager/rpm-alert-receiver:${TAG_MANAGER} ※An error about missing "go" may appear midway, but this does not cause any operational problem.
$ sudo buildah push $HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_manager/resource-pool-manager:${TAG_MANAGER}
$ sudo buildah push $HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_manager/rpm-alert-receiver:${TAG_MANAGER}
$ make helmchart
$ CHART_VERSION=$(yq -r .version ./dist/chart/Chart.yaml)
$ helm push ./dist/resource-pool-manager-${CHART_VERSION}.tgz oci://$HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_manager/

.. Build DCI Physical Infrastructure materials into two types — "container image" and "Helm chart" — and register them to Harbor.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ cd ~
$ git clone -b v${TAG_PHYSICAL} https://github.com/openkasugai/dci_physical_infrastructure
$ cd dci_physical_infrastructure
$ vi cdi/Dockerfile #If a proxy is required for external communication, add proxy settings (refer to "6. Proxy settings content for Dockerfile")
$ vi maas/Dockerfile #If a proxy is required for external communication, add proxy settings (refer to "6. Proxy settings content for Dockerfile")
$ vi network/Dockerfile #If a proxy is required for external communication, add proxy settings (refer to "6. Proxy settings content for Dockerfile")
$ vi exporter/Dockerfile #If a proxy is required for external communication, add proxy settings (refer to "6. Proxy settings content for Dockerfile")
$ vi log/Dockerfile #If a proxy is required for external communication, add proxy settings (refer to "6. Proxy settings content for Dockerfile")
$ make build-all IMG_TAG=${TAG_PHYSICAL}
$ sudo buildah tag localhost/dci_physical_infrastructure/cdi:${TAG_PHYSICAL} $HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_physical_infrastructure/cdi:${TAG_PHYSICAL}
$ sudo buildah tag localhost/dci_physical_infrastructure/maas:${TAG_PHYSICAL} $HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_physical_infrastructure/maas:${TAG_PHYSICAL}
$ sudo buildah tag localhost/dci_physical_infrastructure/network:${TAG_PHYSICAL} $HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_physical_infrastructure/network:${TAG_PHYSICAL}
$ sudo buildah tag localhost/dci_physical_infrastructure/exporter:${TAG_PHYSICAL} $HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_physical_infrastructure/exporter:${TAG_PHYSICAL}
$ sudo buildah tag localhost/dci_physical_infrastructure/log:${TAG_PHYSICAL} $HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_physical_infrastructure/log:${TAG_PHYSICAL}
$ sudo buildah push $HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_physical_infrastructure/cdi:${TAG_PHYSICAL}
$ sudo buildah push $HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_physical_infrastructure/maas:${TAG_PHYSICAL}
$ sudo buildah push $HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_physical_infrastructure/network:${TAG_PHYSICAL}
$ sudo buildah push $HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_physical_infrastructure/exporter:${TAG_PHYSICAL}
$ sudo buildah push $HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_physical_infrastructure/log:${TAG_PHYSICAL}
$ helm package ./chart/
$ CHART_VERSION=$(yq -r .version ./chart/Chart.yaml)
$ helm push physical-infrastructure-${CHART_VERSION}.tgz oci://$HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_physical_infrastructure/
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
(Container images for each component of dci_physical_infrastructure (cdi, maas, network, exporter, log) are built with the ${TAG_PHYSICAL} tag, and each container image is registered to the dci_physical_infrastructure project in the Harbor registry)
(A Helm chart package for deploying the entire dci_physical_infrastructure to the Kubernetes environment is generated and registered to the OCI Helm Chart repository in the Harbor registry)

.. Build DCI proprietary provider materials into two types — "container image" and "Helm chart" — and register them to Harbor.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ cd ~/dci_manager/cluster-api-provider-dci/
$ vi Dockerfile #If a proxy is required for external communication, add proxy settings (refer to "6. Proxy settings content for Dockerfile")
$ sudo make buildah-build IMG=$HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_manager/cluster-api-provider-dci:${TAG_MANAGER}
$ sudo buildah push $HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_manager/cluster-api-provider-dci:${TAG_MANAGER}
$ make build-local-repository IMG=$HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_manager/cluster-api-provider-dci:${TAG_MANAGER} CAPDCI_VERSION=v${TAG_MANAGER}
$ vi dist/infrastructure-capdci/v${TAG_MANAGER}/metadata.yaml #If metadata.yaml does not contain the major (XX) and minor (YY) version pair from TAG_MANAGER (XX.YY.ZZ), add it.
$ mkdir -p $HOME/.cluster-api/
$ cat <<EOF > $HOME/.cluster-api/clusterctl.yaml
providers:
- name: "capdci"
  url: "$HOME/dci_manager/cluster-api-provider-dci/dist/infrastructure-capdci/latest/infrastructure-components.yaml"
  type: "InfrastructureProvider"

# Uncomment below fields if you want to override Image without changing installer YAML file.
# images:
#   infrastructure-capdci:
#     repository: localhost
#     name: cluster-api-provider-dci
#     tag: dev
EOF
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
(A container image is built from cluster-api-provider-dci and registered to the dci_manager project in the Harbor registry with the ${TAG_MANAGER} tag)
----

.. Build Temporal into two types — "container image" and "Helm chart" — and register them to Harbor.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ cd ~/dci_tenant_infrastructure/app-monitor-dci/temporal/
$ vi workflow/Dockerfile #If a proxy is required for external communication, add proxy settings (refer to "6. Proxy settings content for Dockerfile")
$ vi gateway/Dockerfile #If a proxy is required for external communication, add proxy settings (refer to "6. Proxy settings content for Dockerfile")
$ vi client/Dockerfile #If a proxy is required for external communication, add proxy settings (refer to "6. Proxy settings content for Dockerfile")
$ sudo buildah bud -t $HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_tenant_infrastructure/temporal-workflow:${TAG_TENANT}  -f workflow/Dockerfile
$ sudo buildah bud -t $HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_tenant_infrastructure/temporal-gateway:${TAG_TENANT}  -f gateway/Dockerfile
$ sudo buildah bud -t $HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_tenant_infrastructure/temporal-client:${TAG_TENANT}  -f client/Dockerfile
$ sudo buildah push $HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_tenant_infrastructure/temporal-workflow:${TAG_TENANT}
$ sudo buildah push $HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_tenant_infrastructure/temporal-gateway:${TAG_TENANT}
$ sudo buildah push $HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_tenant_infrastructure/temporal-client:${TAG_TENANT}
$ helm pull temporal --repo https://go.temporal.io/helm-charts/ --version 0.73.1 --untar
($ helm pull temporal --repo https://go.temporal.io/helm-charts/ --version <Temporal Version> --untar)
$ cd temporal/
$ patch -p1 < ../temporal-chart.patch
$ cd ..
$ helm package ./temporal
$ helm push temporal-0.73.1.tgz oci://$HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_tenant_infrastructure/
($ helm push temporal-<Temporal Version>.tgz oci://$HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_tenant_infrastructure/)
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
(Three types of container images — temporal-workflow, temporal-gateway, and temporal-client — are built from the Temporal-related source code in app-monitor-dci with the ${TAG_TENANT} tag and registered to the dci_tenant_infrastructure project in the Harbor registry)
(The official Temporal Helm chart is obtained, a Helm chart package customized for DCI by applying temporal-chart.patch is generated, and registered to the OCI Helm Chart repository in the Harbor registry)

.. (Only if a proxy is required for external communication) Proxy settings to add to Dockerfile
+
Add the following immediately after the "FROM [string that differs per Dockerfile (e.g., docker.io/golang:1.26.2)] AS builder" line in each Dockerfile.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ vi Dockerfile
※ Add the following.
----

[source,bash]
----
ARG http_proxy=http://[プロキシのIPアドレス]:[プロキシのポート番号]/
ARG https_proxy=http://[プロキシのIPアドレス]:[プロキシのポート番号]/
ARG no_proxy=localhost,127.0.0.1,[Management NW CIDR],[Host NW CIDR],[CNI NW CIDR],[Pod CIDR],[Service CIDR],.cluster.local,.svc,kubernetes.default.svc,harbor-core,harbor-database,harbor-jobservice,harbor-nginx,harbor-portal,harbor-redis,harbor-registry,harbor-trivy,[Manager Harbor IP address]
ARG HTTP_PROXY=http://[プロキシのIPアドレス]:[プロキシのポート番号]/
ARG HTTPS_PROXY=http://[プロキシのIPアドレス]:[プロキシのポート番号]/
ARG NO_PROXY=localhost,127.0.0.1,[Management NW CIDR],[Host NW CIDR],[CNI NW CIDR],[Pod CIDR],[Service CIDR],.cluster.local,.svc,kubernetes.default.svc,harbor-core,harbor-database,harbor-jobservice,harbor-nginx,harbor-portal,harbor-redis,harbor-registry,harbor-trivy,[Manager Harbor IP address]
----

. Deploy each component.

.. Create a ConfigMap for the default alert rule settings.

.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ cd ~/dci_manager/others/monitoring/kube-prometheus-stack
$ vi default-alert-settings.yaml
※ Change the email addresses value under default-contact-points.yaml in the file according to your environment.
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
apiVersion: v1
kind: ConfigMap
metadata:
  name: grafana-alerting-provisioning
  namespace: prometheus
data:
  default-alert-rules.yaml: |
    (omitted)

  default-contact-points.yaml: |
    apiVersion: 1
    contactPoints:
        - orgId: 1
          name: email
          receivers:
            - uid: dfaji07p76scgc
              type: email
              settings:
                addresses: hoge@example.com   ★ Edit here
                singleEmail: false
              disableResolveMessage: false
        - orgId: 1
          name: email-and-webhook
          receivers:
            - uid: cfajiybox651da
              type: webhook
              settings:
                httpMethod: POST
                url: http://resource-pool-manager-alert-receiver-service.resource-pool-manager-system.svc.cluster.local:8080/alerts
              disableResolveMessage: false
            - uid: efajiybox651ce
              type: email
              settings:
                addresses: hoge@example.com   ★ Edit here
                singleEmail: false
              disableResolveMessage: false
----
Verify that the content is correct before saving.

.Verification Steps
[source,console]
----

----

.Verification Result

.. Edit mgr_kube-prometheus_stack_values.yaml.
+

.Execution Steps
[source,console]

$ vi mgr_kube-prometheus_stack_values.yaml
※ Change the SMTP server settings (grafana.grafana.ini.smtp.host) used for alert email notifications according to your environment.
※ To disable alert email notifications, change grafana.grafana.ini.smtp.enabled to false.
※ Change grafana.adminPassword.

----
prometheus:
  prometheusSpec:
    (omitted)

prometheusOperator:
  (omitted)

kube-state-metrics:
  (omitted)

nodeExporter:
  (omitted)

grafana:
  enabled: true
  image:
    tag: 13.1.2
  adminUser: admin
  adminPassword: ******　　　　　　　★ Edit here
  (omitted)
  grafana.ini:
    smtp:
      enabled: true　　　　　　　　　★ Edit as needed
      host: smtp.example.com:25      ★ Edit for your environment

    (omitted)

# Create default rules for monitoring the cluster
defaultRules:
  create: false

alertmanager:
  enabled: false

($ helm install loki grafana/loki --version  <Loki Version>  -n loki -f mgr_loki_values.yaml --set memcached.image.repository=docker.io/library/memcached --set memcachedExporter.image.repository=docker.io/prom/memcached-exporter)

[source,console]
----
NAME: loki
LAST DEPLOYED: Thu May  7 15:56:03 2026
NAMESPACE: loki
STATUS: deployed
REVISION: 1
DESCRIPTION: Install complete
NOTES:
***********************************************************************
 Welcome to Grafana Loki
 Chart version: 6.29.0
 Chart Name: loki
 Loki version: 3.4.2
***********************************************************************

** Please be patient while the chart is being deployed **

Tip:

  Watch the deployment status using the command: kubectl get pods -w --namespace loki

If pods are taking too long to schedule make sure pod affinity can be fulfilled in the current cluster.

***********************************************************************
Installed components:
***********************************************************************
* loki

Loki has been deployed as a single binary.
This means a single pod is handling reads and writes. You can scale that pod vertically by adding more CPU and memory resources.


***********************************************************************
Sending logs to Loki
***********************************************************************

Loki has been configured with a gateway (nginx) to support reads and writes from a single component.

You can send logs from inside the cluster using the cluster DNS:

http://loki-gateway.loki.svc.cluster.local/loki/api/v1/push

You can test to send data from outside the cluster by port-forwarding the gateway to your local machine:

  kubectl port-forward --namespace loki svc/loki-gateway 3100:80 &

And then using http://127.0.0.1:3100/loki/api/v1/push URL as shown below:

```
curl -H "Content-Type: application/json" -XPOST -s "http://127.0.0.1:3100/loki/api/v1/push"  \
--data-raw "{\"streams\": [{\"stream\": {\"job\": \"test\"}, \"values\": [[\"$(date +%s)000000000\", \"fizzbuzz\"]]}]}"
```

Then verify that Loki did receive the data using the following command:

```
curl "http://127.0.0.1:3100/loki/api/v1/query_range" --data-urlencode 'query={job="test"}' | jq .data.result
```

***********************************************************************
Connecting Grafana to Loki
***********************************************************************

If Grafana operates within the cluster, you'll set up a new Loki datasource by utilizing the following URL:

http://loki-gateway.loki.svc.cluster.local/
----

    SONiC (NW device): "network_sw"

.. Edit /etc/rsyslog.conf.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ sudoedit /etc/rsyslog.conf
※ Add the following at the bottom of the file.
----

[source,bash]
----
module(load="imtcp")
input(type="imtcp" port="514" ruleset="test")
if $fromhost-ip == "127.0.0.1" then stop
if $fromhost-ip == "::1" then stop

lookup_table(
  name="audit_machine_iplist"
  file="/etc/rsyslog.d/audit_machine_ips.lst"
  reloadOnHUP="on"
)

if lookup("audit_machine_iplist", $fromhost-ip) != ""  then {
    set $!lk_result = lookup("audit_machine_iplist", $fromhost-ip);
    $template LookupBasedLogs,"/var/log/%$!lk_result%/audit/%fromhost-ip%_%$year%%$month%%$day%.log"
    *.* ?LookupBasedLogs
    stop
}

$template RemoteLogs,"/var/log/network_sw/%fromhost-ip%_%$year%%$month%%$day%.log"
*.* ?RemoteLogs
& ~
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
None in particular
----

.Verification Steps
[source,console]
----
$ more /etc/rsyslog.conf
----
