.. Configure the GUI connection settings for Grafana-A (1st set). (The following is one example. Other methods to access the Grafana GUI are also acceptable.)
+
--
.Configuration Procedure
[source,console]
----
The following configures the terminal used to connect by SSH from the operation terminal (DCI Operator). (Tera Term example)
- Select Terminal settings -> SSH Forwarding to open the SSH Port Forwarding screen, then click "Add"
- On the SSH Port Forwarding settings screen, set the local port to "3000", the remote host to "VIP for DCI Management Cluster Control Plane (e.g., 172.28.193.30)", and the remote port to "30100", then click "OK".
- Press "OK" on the SSH Port Forwarding start screen.
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
Accessing http://localhost:3000/ from a browser allows connection to the Grafana GUI.
- Username: admin
- Password: ******
----
--

.. Configure the GUI connection settings for Grafana-B (2nd set).
+
--
.Configuration Procedure
[source,console]
----
The following configures the terminal used to connect by SSH from the operation terminal (DCI Operator). (Tera Term example)
- Select Terminal settings -> SSH Forwarding to open the SSH Port Forwarding screen, then click "Add"
- On the SSH Port Forwarding settings screen, set the local port to "3001", the remote host to "VIP for DCI Management Cluster Control Plane (e.g., 172.28.193.30)", and the remote port to "30001", then click "OK".
- Press "OK" on the SSH Port Forwarding start screen.
----

==== CA/Issuer Creation for Physical Infrastructure gRPC mTLS `@control plane`

Prerequisite: Each custom-developed component of the physical infrastructure (CDI control/MAAS control/NW control) uses a common CA (Certificate Authority). Even when there are multiple physical infrastructures, all use the same common CA.

. Create a CA key file and CA crt file.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ mkdir -p ~/pki/shared-root-ca
$ cd ~/pki/shared-root-ca
$ openssl genrsa -out ca.key 4096
$ openssl req -x509 -new -nodes -key ca.key -sha256 -days 3650 \
    -subj "/C=JP/ST=Tokyo/O=Example/OU=Platform/CN=shared-root-ca" \
    -out ca.crt
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
None in particular
----

.Verification Steps
[source,console]
----
None in particular
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
None in particular
----
--

. Create a CA Secret.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ kubectl -n cert-manager create secret tls shared-root-ca-keypair \
  --cert=ca.crt \
  --key=ca.key
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
secret/shared-root-ca-keypair created
----

.Verification Steps
[source,console]
----
None in particular
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
None in particular
----
--

. Create a ClusterIssuer.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ cat <<'EOF' | kubectl apply -f -
apiVersion: cert-manager.io/v1
kind: ClusterIssuer
metadata:
  name: shared-root-ca
spec:
  ca:
    secretName: shared-root-ca-keypair
EOF
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
clusterissuer.cert-manager.io/shared-root-ca created
----

.Verification Steps
[source,console]
----
kubectl get clusterissuer shared-root-ca
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
NAME             READY   AGE
shared-root-ca   True    40s
----
--

==== Resource Pool Management Deployment `@control plane`

. Edit cloud-config.yaml.
+
※ For each site (location), create and apply the following ConfigMap manifest describing cloud-init commands to execute for VM hosts (server-type: host ※ fixed) and spare nodes (server-type: spare ※ fixed). Describe the commands required for external connection such as dnf install.
+
※ Modify nameserver, rhc activation-key/organization, and NTP server according to your environment.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ cd ~/dci_manager/resource-pool-manager/
$ vi cloud-config.yaml
----

[source,bash]
----
apiVersion: v1
kind: ConfigMap
metadata:
  name: cloud-config
  namespace: dcirpm
data:
  cloud-configs: |
    - server-type: host
      location: dc1
      body: |
        #cloud-config
        runcmd:
        - "echo 'nameserver 8.8.8.8\nsearch maas' > /etc/resolv.conf" #"echo 'nameserver <DNS-SV-IPAddr>\nsearch maas' > /etc/resolv.conf"
        - "sed -i '/^proxy=/d' /etc/yum.conf"
        - "sed -i '/^proxy=/d' /etc/dnf/dnf.conf"
        - |
          for i in $(seq 1 10); do
            if rhc connect --activation-key=xxxx --organization=12345678; then #"rhc connect --activation-key=<RHC-activation-key> --organization=<RHC-organization>"
              echo "rhc connect succeeded on attempt $i"
              break
            fi

            if [ "$i" -eq 10 ]; then
              echo "rhc connect failed after 10 attempts"
              exit 1
            fi

            echo "rhc connect failed on attempt $i, retrying in 5 seconds..."
            sleep 5
          done
    - server-type: host
      location: dc2
      body: |
        #cloud-config
        runcmd:
        - "echo 'nameserver 8.8.8.8\nsearch maas' > /etc/resolv.conf" #"echo 'nameserver <DNS-SV-IPAddr>\nsearch maas' > /etc/resolv.conf"
        - "sed -i '/^proxy=/d' /etc/yum.conf"
        - "sed -i '/^proxy=/d' /etc/dnf/dnf.conf"
        - |
          for i in $(seq 1 10); do
            if rhc connect --activation-key=xxxx --organization=12345678; then #"rhc connect --activation-key=<RHC-activation-key> --organization=<RHC-organization>"
              echo "rhc connect succeeded on attempt $i"
              break
            fi

            if [ "$i" -eq 10 ]; then
              echo "rhc connect failed after 10 attempts"
              exit 1
            fi

            echo "rhc connect failed on attempt $i, retrying in 5 seconds..."
            sleep 5
          done
    - server-type: spare
      location: dc1
      body: |
        #cloud-config
        runcmd:
          - "echo -e \"nameserver 8.8.8.8 8.8.4.4\\nsearch maas\" > /etc/resolv.conf" #"echo 'nameserver <DNS-SV-IPAddr>\nsearch maas' > /etc/resolv.conf"
          - "sed -i '/^proxy=/d' /etc/yum.conf"
          - "sed -i '/^proxy=/d' /etc/dnf/dnf.conf"
          - |
            for i in $(seq 1 10); do
              if rhc connect --activation-key=xxxx --organization=12345678; then #"rhc connect --activation-key=<RHC-activation-key> --organization=<RHC-organization>"
                echo "rhc connect succeeded on attempt $i"
                break
              fi

              if [ "$i" -eq 10 ]; then
                echo "rhc connect failed after 10 attempts"
                exit 1
              fi

              echo "rhc connect failed on attempt $i, retrying in 5 seconds..."
              sleep 5
            done
          - "dnf install -y wget"
          - "wget https://github.com/mikefarah/yq/releases/latest/download/yq_linux_amd64 -O /usr/bin/yq && chmod +x /usr/bin/yq"
          - "swapoff -a"
          - "sed -i '/swap/ s/^/#/' /etc/fstab"
          - "dnf install -y git"
          - "dnf install -y containers-common"
          - "export KUBERNETES_VERSION=v1.36" #"export KUBERNETES_VERSION=<K8S Version>"
          - "echo [kubernetes] >> /etc/yum.repos.d/kubernetes.repo"
          - "echo name=Kubernetes >> /etc/yum.repos.d/kubernetes.repo"
          - "echo baseurl=https://pkgs.k8s.io/core:/stable:/$KUBERNETES_VERSION/rpm/ >> /etc/yum.repos.d/kubernetes.repo"
          - "echo enabled=1 >> /etc/yum.repos.d/kubernetes.repo"
          - "echo gpgcheck=1 >> /etc/yum.repos.d/kubernetes.repo"
          - "echo gpgkey=https://pkgs.k8s.io/core:/stable:/$KUBERNETES_VERSION/rpm/repodata/repomd.xml.key >> /etc/yum.repos.d/kubernetes.repo"
          - "dnf install -y kubelet-1.36.3-150500.1.1.x86_64 kubeadm-1.36.3-150500.1.1.x86_64 kubectl-1.36.3-150500.1.1.x86_64" #"dnf install -y kubelet-<K8S Package>.x86_64 kubeadm-<K8S Package>.x86_64 kubectl-<K8S Package>.x86_64"
          - "export CRIO_VERSION=v1.36" #"export CRIO_VERSION=<CRI-O Version>"
          - "echo [cri-o] >> /etc/yum.repos.d/cri-o.repo"
          - "echo name=CRI-O >> /etc/yum.repos.d/cri-o.repo"
          - "echo baseurl=https://download.opensuse.org/repositories/isv:/cri-o:/stable:/$CRIO_VERSION/rpm/ >> /etc/yum.repos.d/cri-o.repo"
          - "echo enabled=1 >> /etc/yum.repos.d/cri-o.repo"
          - "echo gpgcheck=1 >> /etc/yum.repos.d/cri-o.repo"
          - "echo gpgkey=https://download.opensuse.org/repositories/isv:/cri-o:/stable:/$CRIO_VERSION/rpm/repodata/repomd.xml.key >> /etc/yum.repos.d/cri-o.repo"
          - "dnf install -y cri-o-1.36.3-150500.1.1.x86_64" #"dnf install -y cri-o-<CRI-O Package>.x86_64"
          - "setenforce 0"
          - "sed -i 's/enforcing/permissive/g' /etc/selinux/config"
          - "dnf install -y nfs-utils-2.5.4-34.el9.x86_64" #"dnf install -y nfs-utils-<NFS-utils Version>.x86_64"
          - "echo \"spare node setup done!\""
        ntp:
          enabled: true
          servers:
            - 172.28.197.80 #<NTP-SV-IPAddr>
----
Verify that the content is correct before saving.

. Edit dist/chart/values.yaml.

※ This depends on the physical infrastructure configuration; in this document, as a configuration example, delete the phy2 element from the infrastructures array and keep only the phy1 element.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ vi dist/chart/values.yaml
----

[source,bash]
----
config:
  configYaml: |-
    db:
      host: database-cluster-rw.database.svc.cluster.local
      port: 5432
      dbname: app
      username: postgres
      secretRef:
        name: database-cluster-app
        namespace: database
    infrastructures:
      - name: phy1
        maas:
          host: physical-infrastructure-maas-service.default.svc.cluster.local
          port: 82
        nw:
          host: physical-infrastructure-network-service.default.svc.cluster.local
          port: 81
        cdi:
          host: physical-infrastructure-cdi-service.default.svc.cluster.local
          port: 80
    default:
      location: dc1
      os:
        distribution: rhel
        version: "9.6-custom"
      cdiGrpName: group0
----

. Install resource-pool-manager using helm.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]

postgres@database-cluster-1:/$ psql -U postgres -d app
app=# CREATE ROLE api_user;
app=# GRANT USAGE ON SCHEMA public TO api_user;
app=# GRANT CONNECT ON DATABASE app TO api_user;
app=# GRANT SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE ON ALL TABLES IN SCHEMA public TO api_user;
app=# exit
postgres@database-cluster-1:/$ exit

$ export DB_PASSWORD=$(kubectl -n database get secrets database-cluster-app -o jsonpath='{.data.password}' | base64 -d)
$ export DB_URI=$(echo -n "postgres://postgres:$DB_PASSWORD@database-cluster-rw.database.svc.cluster.local:5432/app?sslmode=require" | base64 -w 0)
$ export JWT_SECRET=$(< /dev/urandom tr -dc A-Za-z0-9 | head -c32)
$ export JWT_SECRET_BASE64=$(echo -n $JWT_SECRET | base64 -w 0)
----

==== ClusterAPI Installation `@control plane`

. Install the clusterctl command.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ cd ~/
$ export CAPI_VERSION=v1.14.0
$ curl -L https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/cluster-api/releases/download/${CAPI_VERSION}/clusterctl-linux-amd64 -o clusterctl
$ sudo install -o root -g root -m 0755 clusterctl /usr/local/bin/clusterctl
----

. Install the Cluster API providers.

[arabic]
.. Create and edit the Core Provider yaml file.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ clusterctl generate provider --core cluster-api:${CAPI_VERSION} > core.yaml
$ vi core.yaml
※ Add nodeSelector and the tolerations operator under spec.template.spec of kind: Deployment for capi-controller-manager.
※ The content to add is as follows. (Only Deployment is extracted.)
----

[source,bash]
----
---
apiVersion: apps/v1
kind: Deployment
metadata:
  labels:
    cluster.x-k8s.io/provider: cluster-api
    clusterctl.cluster.x-k8s.io: ""
    control-plane: controller-manager
  name: capi-controller-manager
  namespace: capi-system
spec:
  replicas: 1
  selector:
    matchLabels:
      cluster.x-k8s.io/provider: cluster-api
      control-plane: controller-manager
  strategy: {}
  template:
    metadata:
      labels:
        cluster.x-k8s.io/provider: cluster-api
        control-plane: controller-manager
    spec:
      nodeSelector:　　　　　　　　　　　　　　　　   ★ Add
        node-role.kubernetes.io/control-plane: ""　   ★ Add
      containers:
      - args:
        - --leader-elect
        - --diagnostics-address=:8443
        - --insecure-diagnostics=false
        - --feature-gates=MachinePool=true,ClusterTopology=false,RuntimeSDK=false,MachineSetPreflightChecks=true,MachineWaitForVolumeDetachConsiderVolumeAttachments=true,PriorityQueue=false,ReconcilerRateLimiting=false,InPlaceUpdates=false,MachineTaintPropagation=false
        command:
        - /manager
        env:
        - name: POD_NAMESPACE
          valueFrom:
            fieldRef:
              fieldPath: metadata.namespace
        - name: POD_NAME
          valueFrom:
            fieldRef:
              fieldPath: metadata.name
        - name: POD_UID
          valueFrom:
            fieldRef:
              fieldPath: metadata.uid
        image: registry.k8s.io/cluster-api/cluster-api-controller:v1.14.0
        imagePullPolicy: IfNotPresent
        livenessProbe:
          httpGet:
            path: /healthz
            port: healthz
        name: manager
        ports:
        - containerPort: 9443
          name: webhook-server
          protocol: TCP
        - containerPort: 9440
          name: healthz
          protocol: TCP
        - containerPort: 8443
          name: metrics
          protocol: TCP
        readinessProbe:
RollingUpdateStrategy:  25% max unavailable, 25% max surge
        volumeMounts:
        - mountPath: /tmp/k8s-webhook-server/serving-certs
          name: cert
          readOnly: true
      securityContext:
        runAsNonRoot: true
        seccompProfile:
          type: RuntimeDefault
      serviceAccountName: capi-manager
      terminationGracePeriodSeconds: 10
      tolerations:
      - effect: NoSchedule
        key: node-role.kubernetes.io/master
      - effect: NoSchedule
        operator: "Exists"　    ★ Add
        key: node-role.kubernetes.io/control-plane
      volumes:
      - name: cert
        secret:
          secretName: capi-webhook-service-cert
status: {}

$ vi bootstrap.yaml
※ The content to add is the same as core.yaml.

----

[source,bash]
----
---
apiVersion: apps/v1
kind: Deployment
metadata:
  labels:
    cluster.x-k8s.io/provider: bootstrap-kubeadm
    clusterctl.cluster.x-k8s.io: ""
    control-plane: controller-manager
  name: capi-kubeadm-bootstrap-controller-manager
  namespace: capi-kubeadm-bootstrap-system
spec:
  replicas: 1
  selector:
    matchLabels:
      cluster.x-k8s.io/provider: bootstrap-kubeadm
      control-plane: controller-manager
  strategy: {}
  template:
    metadata:
      labels:
        cluster.x-k8s.io/provider: bootstrap-kubeadm
        control-plane: controller-manager
    spec:
      nodeSelector:　　　　　　　　　　　　　　　　   ★ Add
        node-role.kubernetes.io/control-plane: ""　   ★ Add
      containers:
      - args:
        - --leader-elect
        - --diagnostics-address=:8443
        - --insecure-diagnostics=false
        - --feature-gates=MachinePool=true,KubeadmBootstrapFormatIgnition=false,PriorityQueue=false,ReconcilerRateLimiting=false
        - --bootstrap-token-ttl=24h     　★ Change
        command:
        - /manager
        env:
        - name: POD_NAMESPACE
          valueFrom:
            fieldRef:
              fieldPath: metadata.namespace
        - name: POD_NAME
          valueFrom:
            fieldRef:
              fieldPath: metadata.name
        - name: POD_UID
          valueFrom:
            fieldRef:
              fieldPath: metadata.uid
        image: registry.k8s.io/cluster-api/kubeadm-bootstrap-controller:v1.14.0
        imagePullPolicy: IfNotPresent
        livenessProbe:
          httpGet:
            path: /healthz
            port: healthz
        name: manager
        ports:
        - containerPort: 9443
          name: webhook-server
          protocol: TCP
        - containerPort: 9440
          name: healthz
          protocol: TCP
        - containerPort: 8443
          name: metrics
          protocol: TCP
        readinessProbe:
          httpGet:
            path: /readyz
            port: healthz
        resources: {}
        securityContext:
          allowPrivilegeEscalation: false
          capabilities:
            drop:
            - ALL
          privileged: false
          runAsGroup: 65532
          runAsUser: 65532
        terminationMessagePolicy: FallbackToLogsOnError
        volumeMounts:
        - mountPath: /tmp/k8s-webhook-server/serving-certs
          name: cert
          readOnly: true
      securityContext:
        runAsNonRoot: true
        seccompProfile:
          type: RuntimeDefault
      serviceAccountName: capi-kubeadm-bootstrap-manager
      terminationGracePeriodSeconds: 10
      tolerations:
      - effect: NoSchedule
        key: node-role.kubernetes.io/master
      - effect: NoSchedule
        operator: "Exists"　    ★ Add
        key: node-role.kubernetes.io/control-plane
      volumes:
      - name: cert
        secret:
          secretName: capi-kubeadm-bootstrap-webhook-service-cert
status: {}

.. Create and edit the yaml file for the Add-on Provider.

.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ clusterctl generate provider --addon helm:v0.6.4 > helm-addon.yaml
($ clusterctl generate provider --addon helm:<Add-on Provider Version> > helm-addon.yaml)
$ vi helm-addon.yaml
※ Add nodeSelector and tolerations under spec.template.spec of caaph-controller-manager with kind: Deployment.
※ The content to add is as follows. (Only Deployment is extracted.)
----

----
---
apiVersion: apps/v1
kind: Deployment
metadata:
  labels:
    cluster.x-k8s.io/provider: addon-helm
    clusterctl.cluster.x-k8s.io: ""
    control-plane: controller-manager
  name: caaph-controller-manager
  namespace: caaph-system
spec:
  replicas: 1
  selector:
    matchLabels:
      cluster.x-k8s.io/provider: helm
      control-plane: controller-manager
  strategy: {}
  template:
    metadata:
      annotations:
        kubectl.kubernetes.io/default-container: manager
      labels:
        cluster.x-k8s.io/provider: helm
        control-plane: controller-manager
    spec:
      nodeSelector:                                 # Add
        node-role.kubernetes.io/control-plane: ""  # Add
      tolerations:                                  # Add
      - effect: NoSchedule                          # Add
        operator: "Exists"                          # Add
        key: node-role.kubernetes.io/control-plane  # Add
      containers:
      - args:
        - --leader-elect
        - --diagnostics-address=:8443
        - --insecure-diagnostics=false
        - --sync-period=10m
        - --v=2
        env:
        - name: XDG_DATA_HOME
          value: /tmp/xdg/.data
        - name: XDG_CONFIG_HOME
          value: /tmp/xdg/.config
        - name: XDG_STATE_HOME
          value: /tmp/xdg/.state
        - name: XDG_CACHE_HOME
          value: /tmp/xdg/.cache
        - name: XDG_CONFIG_DIRS
          value: /tmp/xdg
        - name: KUBECACHEDIR
          value: /tmp/.kube/cache
        image: registry.k8s.io/cluster-api-helm/cluster-api-helm-controller:v0.6.4
        imagePullPolicy: IfNotPresent
        livenessProbe:
          httpGet:
            path: /healthz
            port: healthz
          initialDelaySeconds: 10
          periodSeconds: 10
        name: manager
        ports:
        - containerPort: 9443
          name: webhook-server
          protocol: TCP
        - containerPort: 9440
          name: healthz
          protocol: TCP
        - containerPort: 8443
          name: metrics
          protocol: TCP
        readinessProbe:
          httpGet:
            path: /readyz
            port: healthz
          initialDelaySeconds: 10
          periodSeconds: 10
        resources: {}
        securityContext:
          allowPrivilegeEscalation: false
          capabilities:
            drop:
            - ALL
          privileged: false
          readOnlyRootFilesystem: true
          runAsGroup: 65532
          runAsUser: 65532
        volumeMounts:
        - mountPath: /tmp/k8s-webhook-server/serving-certs
          name: cert
          readOnly: true
        - mountPath: /tmp
          name: tmp
      securityContext:
        runAsNonRoot: true
        seccompProfile:
          type: RuntimeDefault
      serviceAccountName: caaph-controller-manager
      terminationGracePeriodSeconds: 10
      volumes:
      - name: cert
        secret:
          defaultMode: 420
          secretName: webhook-server-cert
      - emptyDir: {}
        name: tmp
status: {}
----

==== DCI Proprietary Provider Deployment `@control plane`

Prerequisites: Before executing this section, first execute "4.2. Registration of Resource Information to Configuration Management DB".

. Create the YAML file for the DCI-specific Provider (Infrastructure Provider).
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----

==== Monitored Device Configuration for DCI Management Cluster

===== Prerequisites

In this procedure, AAA.AAA.AAA.AAA and BBB.BBB.BBB.BBB are used as destination IP addresses.

- AAA.AAA.AAA.AAA is the IP address of a specific Control Plane within the K8s Cluster (the IP address of the control plane on which rsyslog was configured in the previous section)
- BBB.BBB.BBB.BBB is the IP address of a specific K8s Node within the K8s Cluster (the IP address of the worker node on which rsyslog was configured in the previous section)

The IP addresses above are the same values for all machines and procedures.
When executing, replace each with the corresponding IP address.

=== DCI Physical Infrastructure Deployment `@control plane`

This section describes the procedure to deploy physical infrastructure components used in DCI.

This section shows the procedure corresponding to the section enclosed in the red box in "2.4. Construction Workflow".

image::media/media/flow6_en.png[width=432]

※ During physical infrastructure deployment, this must be performed after PostgreSQL configuration, as it references the secret (database-cluster-app) created during PostgreSQL setup.

※ In this section, if a procedure fails, restart that procedure from the beginning.

. Generate SSH keys.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ cd ~/
$ ssh-keygen -t ed25519
※ Press Enter (without input) for all prompts to complete generation.
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
Your identification has been saved in /home/cloud-user/.ssh/id_ed25519
Your public key has been saved in /home/cloud-user/.ssh/id_ed25519.pub
The key fingerprint is:
SHA256:xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx cloud-user@host
The key's randomart image is:
+--[ED25519 256]--+
|..E. .o          |
| =...o . .       |
|+o.+  . . o      |
|o.+.o    . .     |
|+ ==.+  S        |
|o=*+o.o          |
|.B=+o            |
|*===             |
|**=..            |
+----[SHA256]-----+
----

.Verification Steps
[source,console]
----
$ ls -l .ssh/id_ed25519*
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
-rw------- 1 cloud-user cloud-user 419 May  8 15:47 .ssh/id_ed25519
-rw-r--r-- 1 cloud-user cloud-user 108 May  8 15:47 .ssh/id_ed25519.pub
----
--

. Generate a secret for SSH key mounting.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ kubectl create secret generic physical-infrastructure-ssh-keys --from-file=id_ed25519=$HOME/.ssh/id_ed25519
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
secret/physical-infrastructure-ssh-keys created
----

.Verification Steps
[source,console]
----
$ kubectl get secret physical-infrastructure-ssh-keys
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
NAME                               TYPE     DATA   AGE
physical-infrastructure-ssh-keys   Opaque   1      95s
----
--

. Install DCI Physical Infrastructure using helm.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ CHART_VERSION=$(yq -r .version ~/dci_physical_infrastructure/chart/Chart.yaml)
$ helm upgrade --install physical-infrastructure oci://$HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_physical_infrastructure/physical-infrastructure \
   --set physical-infrastructure-cdi.image.repository=$HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_physical_infrastructure/cdi \
   --set physical-infrastructure-cdi.image.tag=${TAG_PHYSICAL} \
   --set physical-infrastructure-cdi.tls.enabled=true \
   --set physical-infrastructure-maas.image.repository=$HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_physical_infrastructure/maas \
   --set physical-infrastructure-maas.image.tag=${TAG_PHYSICAL} \
   --set physical-infrastructure-maas.tls.enabled=true \
   --set physical-infrastructure-network.image.repository=$HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_physical_infrastructure/network \
   --set physical-infrastructure-network.image.tag=${TAG_PHYSICAL} \
   --set physical-infrastructure-network.tls.enabled=true \
   --set physical-infrastructure-log.image.repository=$HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_physical_infrastructure/log \
   --set physical-infrastructure-log.image.tag=${TAG_PHYSICAL} \
   --set physical-infrastructure-exporter.image.repository=$HARBOR_MNG:30003/dci_physical_infrastructure/exporter \
   --set physical-infrastructure-exporter.image.tag=${TAG_PHYSICAL} \
   --set global.db.api.accessUrl=https://db-api.local:8443 \
   --version ${CHART_VERSION}
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
Release "physical-infrastructure" does not exist. Installing it now.
Pulled: 192.168.2.127:30003/dci_physical_infrastructure/physical-infrastructure:v0.3.2
Digest: sha256:xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx
192.168.2.127:30003/dci_physical_infrastructure/physical-infrastructure:v0.3.2 contains an underscore.

OCI artifact references (e.g. tags) do not support the plus sign (+). To support
storing semantic versions, Helm adopts the convention of changing plus (+) to
an underscore (_) in chart version tags when pushing to a registry and back to
a plus (+) when pulling from a registry.

=== Component Deployment to Management Cluster

This section describes the procedure to deploy various Components required for DCI operations to the management cluster. +
Specifically, this section describes SSH key configuration and preparation procedures for Components to be deployed to the tenant logical infrastructure.

This section shows the procedure corresponding to the section enclosed in the red box in "2.4. Construction Workflow".

image::media/media/flow7_en.png[width=432]

※ In this section, if a procedure fails, restart that procedure from the beginning.

==== SSH Key Configuration `@control plane`

Register the control plane's SSH key to the CDI management server, CDI management server guest OS, NW switches, and MAAS server. Note: Use the SSH key generated during "DCI Physical Infrastructure Deployment @control plane".

. Register the public key.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ ssh-copy-id -i ~/.ssh/id_ed25519.pub <ユーザ>@<接続先ホスト>
※ The registration target destination hosts are as follows.

==== Register Custom Commissioning Script to Delete Unused USB-NIC Information from MAAS Server

※ USB-NIC refers to a USB-connected network interface card. If not in use, skip this section.

. Create a Python script. Create it on a local PC.
+
The filename shall be "49-remove-usb-nic" (without extension).
+
In EXCLUDED_MAC (list of exclusions from deletion targets), list the MAC addresses of USB-NICs to be used. If there are no USB-NICs to use, set EXCLUDED_MAC = {}.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ vi 49-remove-usb-nic
----

[source,bash]
----
#!/usr/bin/env python3
import json
import os
import pprint

EXCLUDED_MAC = {
    '00:11:22:33:44:55',
    '01:11:22:33:44:55',
}

def main():
    maas_resource_file = os.environ["MAAS_RESOURCES_FILE"]
    with open(maas_resource_file, 'r') as resources_file:
        data=json.load(resources_file)
    resources_file.close()
    filtered_cards = []
    usb_ports = []
    for card in data["resources"]["network"]["cards"]:

        if card['driver'] != 'rndis_host':
            filtered_cards.append(card)
        else:
            if card['ports'][0]['address'] in EXCLUDED_MAC:
                print(f"MAC {card['ports'][0]['address']} is in the excluded list, skipping it")
                filtered_cards.append(card)
                continue
            usb_ports.append(card['ports'][0]['id'])
            print(f"rndis_host found in resources/networks/cards/port, removing it")
    data["resources"]["network"]["cards"]=filtered_cards

    for usb_port in usb_ports:
        if usb_port in data["networks"].keys():
            del(data["networks"][usb_port])
            print(f"Removing interface {usb_port} from the networks elements")
        else:
            print(f"{usb_port} not found, skipping...")

    data_json = json.dumps(data, indent=4)
    with open(maas_resource_file, 'w') as resources_file:
        resources_file.write(data_json)
    resources_file.close()
    pprint.pprint(data["networks"])
    pprint.pprint(data["resources"]["network"]["cards"])

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
None in particular
----

.Verification Steps
[source,console]
----
$ more 49-remove-usb-nic
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
#!/usr/bin/env python3
import json
import os
import pprint

EXCLUDED_MAC = {
    '00:11:22:33:44:55',
    '01:11:22:33:44:55',
}

def main():
    maas_resource_file = os.environ["MAAS_RESOURCES_FILE"]
    with open(maas_resource_file, 'r') as resources_file:
        data=json.load(resources_file)
    resources_file.close()
    filtered_cards = []
    usb_ports = []
    for card in data["resources"]["network"]["cards"]:

        if card['driver'] != 'rndis_host':
            filtered_cards.append(card)
        else:
            if card['ports'][0]['address'] in EXCLUDED_MAC:
                print(f"MAC {card['ports'][0]['address']} is in the excluded list, skipping it")
                filtered_cards.append(card)
                continue
            usb_ports.append(card['ports'][0]['id'])
            print(f"rndis_host found in resources/networks/cards/port, removing it")
    data["resources"]["network"]["cards"]=filtered_cards

    for usb_port in usb_ports:
        if usb_port in data["networks"].keys():
            del(data["networks"][usb_port])
            print(f"Removing interface {usb_port} from the networks elements")
        else:
            print(f"{usb_port} not found, skipping...")

    data_json = json.dumps(data, indent=4)
    with open(maas_resource_file, 'w') as resources_file:
        resources_file.write(data_json)
    resources_file.close()
    pprint.pprint(data["networks"])
    pprint.pprint(data["resources"]["network"]["cards"])

if __name__ == "__main__":

=== DCI Management Cluster System Health Check `@control plane`

This section describes the procedure to verify that the constructed DCI Management Cluster is operating normally.

This section shows the procedure corresponding to the section enclosed in the red box in "2.4. Construction Workflow".

image::media/media/flow8_en.png[width=432]

Verify that all nodes and pods constructed so far are starting normally (Running). +
Note: As a health check, verify with an ansible script that all nodes and pods are starting normally. +
Using this script, if even one node or pod is not Running, the result is NG. +
Also, the following is displayed as a summary in ./ansible_log/result.log as the Execution Result log.

  - Whether the node status is Ready +
  - Pod name, pod image (version), status, reason (if status is Not Running), namespace, deployed node

. Install ansible.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ sudo dnf -y install ansible-core
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
(omitted)
Installed:
  ansible-core-1:2.14.18-1.el9.x86_64      python3-cffi-1.14.5-5.el9.x86_64          python3-cryptography-36.0.1-5.el9_6.x86_64
  python3-packaging-20.9-5.el9.noarch      python3-ply-3.11-14.el9.noarch            python3-pycparser-2.20-6.el9.noarch
  python3-pyparsing-2.4.7-9.el9.noarch     python3-resolvelib-0.5.4-5.el9.noarch     sshpass-1.09-4.el9.x86_64

Complete!
----

.Verification Steps
[source,console]

$ sudo dnf list ansible-core
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
Installed Packages
ansible-core.x86_64                                 1:2.14.18-1.el9                                 @rhel-9-for-x86_64-appstream-rpms
----
--

. Create the ansible script.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ vi node_pod_summary.yaml
※ Copy and paste the following to create the script.
----

[source,bash]
----
---
- name: Kubernetes Node & Pod Summary
  hosts: localhost
  connection: local
  gather_facts: false

  vars:
    log_base_path: "./ansible_log"

  tasks:
    ######################################################################
    # Node Summary
    ######################################################################
    - name: Get nodes summary
      ansible.builtin.command: kubectl get nodes --no-headers
      register: nodes_raw
      changed_when: false

    - name: Build node summary
      ansible.builtin.set_fact:
        node_summary: >-
          {{
            node_summary | default([])
            + [
                {
                  'name': item.split()[0],
                  'status': item.split()[1]
                }
              ]
          }}
      loop: "{{ nodes_raw.stdout_lines }}"

    ######################################################################
    # Pod Summary
    ######################################################################
    - name: Get all pods in JSON format
      ansible.builtin.command: kubectl get pods -A -o json
      register: pods_raw
      changed_when: false

    - name: Build pod summary
      ansible.builtin.set_fact:
        pod_summary: "{{ pod_summary | default([]) + [pod_item] }}"
      vars:
        pod: "{{ item }}"
        phase: "{{ pod.status.phase | default('Unknown') }}"

        # container waiting reason (CrashLoopBackOff, ImagePullBackOff, etc.)
        waiting_reason: >-
          {{
            (
              pod.status.containerStatuses | default([])
              | map(attribute='state.waiting')
              | select('defined')
              | map(attribute='reason')
              | select('defined')
              | list
              | first
            ) | default('')
          }}

        # pod-level reason (Pending, Evicted, etc.)
        pod_reason: "{{ pod.status.reason | default('') }}"

        # final reason
        reason: >-
          {{
            waiting_reason
            if waiting_reason != ''
            else (
              pod_reason
              if pod_reason != ''
              else phase
            )
          }}

        # Treat anything other than Running as Failed
        status: "{{ 'Running' if phase == 'Running' else 'Failed' }}"

        # If nodeName is absent, use NotScheduled
        node: "{{ pod.spec.nodeName | default('NotScheduled') }}"

        pod_item:
          pod: "{{ pod.metadata.name }}"
          image: "{{ pod.spec.containers[0].image }}"
          status: "{{ status }}"
          reason: "{{ reason }}"
          namespace: "{{ pod.metadata.namespace }}"
          node: "{{ node }}"

      loop: "{{ (pods_raw.stdout | from_json)['items'] }}"

    ######################################################################
    # Create log directory
    ######################################################################
    - name: Ensure log directory exists
      ansible.builtin.file:
        path: "{{ log_base_path }}"
        state: directory
        mode: '0755'

    ######################################################################
    # Write result.log
    ######################################################################
    - name: Write summary to log file
      ansible.builtin.copy:
        dest: "{{ log_base_path }}/result.log"
        content: |
          ==== Node Summary ====
          {% for n in node_summary %}
          {{ n.name }}: {{ n.status }}
          {% endfor %}

          ==== Pod Summary ====
          {% for p in pod_summary %}
          pod: {{ p.pod }}
          image: {{ p.image }}
          status: {{ p.status }}
          reason: {{ p.reason }}
          namespace: {{ p.namespace }}
          node: {{ p.node }}

          {% endfor %}
      changed_when: false

    ######################################################################
    # Fail if any pod is Failed
    ######################################################################
    - name: Fail if any pod is Failed
      ansible.builtin.fail:
        msg: "One or more pods are not Running. See {{ log_base_path }}/result.log"
      when: >
        (
          pod_summary
          | selectattr('status', 'equalto', 'Failed')
          | list
          | length
        ) > 0
----
Verify that the content is correct before saving.

.Execution Result

None in particular

----

.Verification Result

----
None (verification is performed while editing the file, so a separate verification is omitted)

==== Pod Summary ====
pod: caaph-controller-manager-79cb648c55-vdgv2
image: registry.k8s.io/cluster-api-helm/cluster-api-helm-controller:v0.6.4
status: Running
reason: Running
namespace: caaph-system

== Initial Resource Setup Procedures for DCI Tenant Logical Infrastructure Construction

This chapter describes the initial configuration procedures and registration procedures for various resources required for constructing DCI Tenant Logical Infrastructure and for increasing/decreasing resources such as "servers, cards, and NW devices".

When increasing or decreasing resources, execute only the procedure corresponding to the target resource. When increasing or decreasing resources other than servers (cards, NW devices), execute only <<configuration_db_resource_registration>>.

=== COTS Server / Compute Server BIOS Configuration

This section describes the BIOS-level initial configuration procedures to be performed before construction for COTS Servers and Compute Servers used in DCI Tenant Logical Infrastructure construction. +
Specifically, perform basic settings required for automated construction and resource management, such as enabling the Redfish API, enabling SR-IOV, and configuring PXE boot.

This section shows the procedure corresponding to the section enclosed in the red box in "2.4. Construction Workflow".

image::media/media/flow41_en.png[width=432]

※ In this section, if a procedure fails, restart that procedure from the beginning.

==== IPMI over LAN Enable

In this procedure, enable IPMI over LAN on the target server to ensure the power information retrieval function of DCI-2.0 operates correctly.
IPMI over LAN is a function for remotely managing server power and status over a network.
In DCI-2.0, this function is used to verify the state of Nodes.

The configuration procedure is shown below, using a Dell PowerEdge R740 server as an example.

. Log in to the Integrated Dell Remote Access Controller (iDRAC) ※. +
※ iDRAC: A dedicated remote management controller included as standard in Dell servers.
+
--
Enter the username and password and press Submit.

image::media/media/1iDRAC9_Login.png[width=1000]
--
. Enable IPMI over LAN.
+
--
After logging in, navigate to "iDRAC Settings → Connectivity → Network → IPMI Settings", set "IPMI Over LAN" to Enabled, and press Apply.

image::media/media/iDRAC9_IPMIoverLAN.png[width=1000]
--

==== Redfish API Enable
※ Execute this procedure only for servers that support Redfish API (if the corresponding setting item exists).

In this procedure, enable the Redfish API on the target server to ensure the node health monitoring function of DCI-2.0 operates correctly.
The Redfish API is a function for externally monitoring server status (startup, shutdown, response availability, etc.).
In DCI-2.0, this function is used to verify the state of Nodes.

. Log in to the Integrated Dell Remote Access Controller (iDRAC).

. Enable Redfish.
+
--
After logging in, navigate to "iDRAC Settings → Services → Redfish", set "Redfish" to Enabled, and press Apply.

image::media/media/iDRAC9_Redfish.png[width=1000]
--

==== SR-IOV Enable

In DCI-2.0, SR-IOV (Single Root I/O Virtualization) is used to share a single physical NIC among multiple Pods. +
In this procedure, enable SR-IOV in the BIOS settings.

. To enter the BIOS screen, first restart the server.
+
--
Connect to iDRAC, navigate to "Configuration → Virtual Console", and press "Start the Virtual Console".

image::media/media/3iDRAC9_StartVirtualConsole-1.png[width=1000]

In the Virtual Console, apply "Console Controls → Ctrl-Alt-Del" to restart the server.

image::media/media/7iDRAC9_ConsoleControls.png[width=1000]
--
. Enter the BIOS screen while the server is booting and enable SR-IOV.
+
--
After the server restarts, when the startup screen (screen before OS boot) with the manufacturer logo appears, press F2 to enter the BIOS screen (System Setup). +
Then, in the BIOS screen, navigate to "System Setup → System BIOS → Integrated Devices" and change "SR-IOV Global Enable" to Enabled.

image::media/media/BIOS_SR-IOV.png[width=1000]
--

. Exit the BIOS screen after enabling SR-IOV.

==== PXE Boot Configuration

To execute PXE boot from MAAS on COTS Servers and CDI Compute Servers, the NIC connected to the Manager NW must be configured at the top priority in the server BIOS boot settings.

. In the BIOS Boot settings screen, change the Manager NW NIC to the first boot device.
+
--
image::media/media/BIOS_BootSequence.png[width=1000]
--

. Exit the BIOS screen after configuring PXE boot.

This completes "COTS Server / Compute Server BIOS Configuration".

[[configuration_db_resource_registration]]
=== Configuration Management DB Resource Information Registration `@control plane`
This section describes the procedure to register information about various resources used in DCI Tenant Logical Infrastructure to the Configuration Management DB. Also register server information planned to be used as VM hosts.

This section shows the procedure corresponding to the section enclosed in the red box in "2.4. Construction Workflow".

image::media/media/flow42_en.png[width=432]

The Go code (main.go) created in this section to register information in the Configuration Management DB can also be generated using the support tools under `dci_common/script/support_tools/pre_setup_tools`. +
However, these are reference tools verified only in specific environments. Depending on your environment, you may need to review or modify the generated results. +
The support tool names and summaries are shown below. For usage and other details, refer to each tool's README. +

[cols="1,3", options="header"]
|===
|Tool Name |Overview

|data_collect_tools
| A support tool that collects server hardware information (specifications, NICs, and GPUs), CDI resource information, and other data to automatically generate the config.yaml required by the Configuration Management DB registration code generator (configuration_management_database)

|configuration_management_database
| A support tool that generates the Go code (main.go) used for initial information registration in the Configuration Management DB. It reads the infrastructure information in config.yaml and automatically generates main.go
|===

==== Registration to Configuration Management DB

. Navigate to the following directory.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ cd ~/dci_manager/others/configuration-management-database/init-mgmt-db
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
None in particular
----

.Verification Steps
[source,console]
----
$ pwd
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
/home/cloud-user/dci_manager/others/configuration-management-database/init-mgmt-db
----
--

. Edit the registration processing for each table in init-mgmt-db/main.go to suit the environment.
+
※ For each table, refer to Appendix 5.7, Configuration Management DB Parameter List.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ vi main.go
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
None in particular
----

.Verification Steps
[source,console]
----
None in particular
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
None in particular
----
--

. (Optional) Perform a trial DB registration using the edited main.go
+
※ Before registering data in the Configuration Management DB, you can validate main.go by registering data in a trial DB in a locally started PostgreSQL container.
+
※ Prerequisites: Docker is installed, and the user performing this procedure can execute docker commands without sudo.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ make initdb-dryrun
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
(This produces the same result as make initdb in step 5 below. If main.go contains an error, an error message is displayed.)
----
--

. In a separate terminal, execute port forwarding to access PostgreSQL on k8s.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ kubectl port-forward -n database svc/database-cluster-rw 5432:5432 --address 0.0.0.0
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
Forwarding from 0.0.0.0:5432 -> 5432
----

.Verification Steps
[source,console]
----
(Can make initdb in the next section succeed?)
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
(If successful, it can be said that PostgreSQL is now accessible)
----
--

. Execute the following to create the init-mgmt-db executable and register data to the Configuration Management DB.
+
※ If main.go contains an error, the operation is automatically rolled back and no data is registered in the DB. Correct main.go and execute it again.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ export PG_PASSWORD=$(kubectl get secrets -n database database-cluster-app -o jsonpath='{.data.password}' | base64 -d)
$ make initdb
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
go run . \
        --password="xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx"
password: xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx

. NW devices, servers, CDI, MAAS information: Add to physical-infrastructure-product-type-mappings
+
Check the contents with kubectl describe cm physical-infrastructure-product-type-mappings, and if there is no entry in physical-infrastructure-product-type-mappings that matches the device information confirmed in the Configuration Management DB above with the field values for each device type (key), add it and restart the physical infrastructure pods.
+
· NW devices (nw_products): vendor, product_name, os +
· Servers (server_products): vendor, product_name +
· CDI (cdi_products): vendor, product_name, version +
· MAAS (maas_products): vendor, product_name, version

. Add to physical-infrastructure-product-type-mappings and restart physical infrastructure pods.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ kubectl edit cm physical-infrastructure-product-type-mappings
※ Add new devices to mapping.json below. The "type" value represents the processing type. For details, check the source in the dci_physical_infrastructure repository.
----

[source,yaml]
----
data:
  mappings.json: |
    {
      "nw_products": [
        {"vendor": "edgecore networks", "product_name": "DCS208/AS5812-54X", "os": "Edgecore SONiC", "type": "EdgeCoreSonic"},
        {"vendor": "edgecore networks", "product_name": "AS4625-54T", "os": "Edgecore SONiC", "type": "EdgeCoreSonic"},
        {"vendor": "edgecore networks", "product_name": "AS7726-32X", "os": "Edgecore SONiC", "type": "EdgeCoreSonic"},

. Devices (GPU/NIC) installed in PG-CDI PCIe-Box: Add to physical-infrastructure-cdi-pg-cdi-model-mappings
+
For resources registered to the t_cdi_gpu and t_cdi_nic tables in the Configuration Management DB using main.go, if the correspondence information between the PG-CDI resource model and the resources in PG-CDI is not described in physical-infrastructure-cdi-pg-cdi-model-mappings, add it and restart the physical infrastructure pods.
+
Verify the model of resources in PG-CDI by logging in to the PG-CDI client Linux-PC with cdictl login and displaying the resource list (e.g., cdictl resource list -f 1 -o JSON) and resource detail information (e.g., cdictl resource show -f 1 -r gpu1 -o JSON).
The following shows an example of confirming the model of gpu1 (A100 PCIe 80GB). The value of resspec_model "a100" below is the model value in CDI.
+
--
.Verification Steps
[source,console]
----
$ cdictl -u $URL auth login
Enter username: xxxx
Password for xxxx:
Login successful.
cdi: cdictl resource show -f 1 -r gpu1 -o JSON
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
Success
{
    "res_uuid": "0fc7a39f-06b8-494c-8422-a9cbeb799f04",
    "res_id_nonliqid": null,
    "fabr_gid": "0x3900",
    "res_type": "gpu",
    "res_name": "gpu1",
    "res_status": 4,
    "res_op_status": "0",
    "res_owner": "n/a",
    "res_serial_num": "n/a",
    "resspecs": [
        {
            "resspec_uuid": "0cad6a10-997d-4f80-bf70-81b34ee50002",
            "resspec_productname": "A100 PCIe 80GB",
            "resspec_model": "a100",
            "resspec_vendor": "NVIDIA Corporation",
            "resspec_removable": false
        }
    ],
    "grp_uuid": "ab7a5e08-e237-4727-bbb3-9a6f092e9d8f",
    "premach_uuid": null,
    "mach_uuid": "942c57a7-e100-4c92-91d8-396bf2eab534",
    "bootimg_uuid": null,
    "lanports": []
}
cdi:
----
--
+
If the model value of the CDI resource confirmed above is not included as a key in mappings.json of physical-infrastructure-cdi-pg-cdi-model-mappings, add it. The value can be any string, but it is recommended to follow the format {vendor}-{product name}-PCIE-{GPU memory size} (all uppercase, hyphen-separated) in line with existing entries. Also, the value string corresponds to the string specified in DCIMachineTemplate in the tenant construction yaml, or in NodeScalingRequest or CardScalingRequest for Node/Card addition requests.
+
. Add to physical-infrastructure-cdi-pg-cdi-model-mappings and restart physical infrastructure pods.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ kubectl edit cm physical-infrastructure-cdi-pg-cdi-model-mappings
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----

. Uninstall PostgreSQL.

----
$ helm uninstall -n database database
----

----

None in particular
----

.Verification Result

None in particular

. Re-execute the Configuration Management DB registration (after modifying main.go).

- TCotsServerPool structure -> t_cots_server_pool table

[[sec_component_list]]
=== Component List
The list of materials used in this manual is shown below.

[[material_list]]
.Component List
|===
|Material Name|Notes
|dci_manager|Core component that comprehensively manages DCI Physical Infrastructure and DCI Tenant Logical Infrastructure
|dci_tenant_infrastructure|Provides the execution environment for DCI Application and logical management functions shared across tenants
|dci_physical_infrastructure|Physical infrastructure consisting of Resource Pool (COTS Server groups, CDI Devices, Physical NW) and management controllers
|dci_common|Contains manuals, scripts, and stub code as I/F implementation examples required for construction and operations

=== Configuration Management DB Parameter List

The following figure shows the relationship between each Configuration Management DB table and the physical configuration.

.Relationship Between Configuration Management DB Tables and Physical Configuration
image::media/media/db_en.png[width=1200]

{empty} +
{empty} +

.Configuration Management DB Parameter List
[cols="1,1,3", options="header"]
|===
|Table Name|Parameter|Description

|t_physical
|name
|DCI Physical Infrastructure Name

|t_location
|location
|Site Name

|t_maas
|access_url
|MAAS Server URL

|
|api_key
|MAAS server API key ※ Set the API key displayed in the procedure in "3.2.2.7. Registering a RHEL Image with MAAS" of this manual

|
|product_info
|Version, etc.

|t_cots_server_pool
|location
|Site Name

|
|host_name
|Hostname

|
|ipmi_address
|IPMI IP Address

|
|ipmi_user
|IPMI Login Username

|
|ipmi_password
|IPMI Login Password

|
|server_role
|0: K8s Control Plane, 1: K8s Node

|
|deployment_type
|0: VM host, 1: Bare metal

|
|mgr_mac_address
|Manager NIC MAC Address

|
|host_mac_address
|Host NIC MAC Address

|
|cni_mac_address
|CNI NIC MAC Address

|
|apldata_mac_address
|Application Data NIC MAC Address

|
|host_nw_port_id
|Set the port of the switch (NW equipment) to which the Host NIC is connected. Specifically, set the primary key (id) value (*1) of the record in the t_nw_port table that corresponds to the switch and port
(*1) The primary key (id) value of the t_nw_port table is a number starting at 1 that is assigned automatically when the record is registered

|
|cni_nw_port_id
|Set the port of the switch (NW equipment) to which the CNI NIC is connected. Specifically, set the primary key (id) value (*1) of the record in the t_nw_port table that corresponds to the switch and port
(*1) The primary key (id) value of the t_nw_port table is a number starting at 1 that is assigned automatically when the record is registered

|
|apldata_nw_port_id
|Set the port of the switch (NW equipment) to which the Application Data NIC is connected. Specifically, set the primary key (id) value (*1) of the record in the t_nw_port table that corresponds to the switch and port
(*1) The primary key (id) value of the t_nw_port table is a number starting at 1 that is assigned automatically when the record is registered

|
|apldata_nic_model
|Application Data NIC Model

|
|cpu_cores
|CPU Core Count

|
|memory_mb
|Memory Size (MB)

|
|storage_gb
|Storage Size (GB)

|
|status
|0: Normal, 1: Failure in progress, 2: Under maintenance

|
|product_info
|Product Identification Information

|t_cots_server_gpu
|server_id
|COTS ServerID

|
|uuid
|GPU UUID

|
|model
|GPU Model

|
|status
|0: Normal, 1: Failure in progress, 2: Under maintenance

|t_cdi
|cdi_name
|CDI Name

|
|location
|Site Name

|
|remote_host
|Client Linux-PC connection destination host

|
|remote_user
|Client Linux-PC login user

|
|group_name
|Group Name

|
|status
|0: Normal, 1: Failure in progress, 2: Under maintenance

|
|product_info
|Product Identification Information

|
|extra_parameters
|- "cdi_guest": Connection destination of CDI management software (guest) +
- "cdi_user": CDI management software login user (user in cdictl -u $URL auth login) +
- "cdi_password": CDI management software login password (password in cdictl -u $URL auth login) +
- "cdimgr_guest_user": CDI management server (guest) username +
- "cdimgr_guest_password": CDI management server (guest) password +
- "cdimgr_host": CDI management server (host) connection destination +
- "cdimgr_host_user": CDI management server (host) username +
- "cdimgr_host_password": CDI management server (host) password +
- "director_password": Director password (password for liqadmin which exists by default)

|t_cdi_compute_pool
|host_name
|Hostname

|
|ipmi_address
|IPMI IP Address

|
|ipmi_user
|IPMI Login Username

|Set the port of the switch (NW equipment) to which the Host NIC is connected. Specifically, set the primary key (id) value (*1) of the record in the t_nw_port table that corresponds to the switch and port
(*1) The primary key (id) value of the t_nw_port table is a number starting at 1 that is assigned automatically when the record is registered

=== Construction Using Automation Scripts

The procedures shown in sections 3.3 through 3.7 can also be performed using automation scripts. +
The automation script names and automation overview are shown below. +
For usage instructions for automation scripts, refer to the "Automation Script Manual".

[cols="1,3", options="header"]
|===
|Script Name|Automation Overview

|manager_cluster_setup.sh
|3.3 DCI Management Cluster Construction +
  3.4 Pre-Setup for DCI Manager and DCI Physical Infrastructure Deployment

|manager_software_deploy.sh
|3.5 DCI Manager Deployment +
  3.6 DCI Physical Infrastructure Deployment +
  3.7 Component Deployment to DCI Management Cluster
|===

[[dci_management_cluster_vm_setup]]
=== DCI Management Cluster Construction Procedure in VM Environment
This chapter describes the procedure for constructing DCI Management Cluster in a virtual machine (VM) environment.

.Prerequisites
* A KVM guest image file must be available. Example: rhel-9.6-x86++_++64-kvm.qcow2

==== VM Host Configuration
===== VM Environment Installation

. To construct a virtualization environment, install QEMU/KVM, libvirt, and virt-install.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ sudo dnf -y install qemu-kvm libvirt virt-install
----

.Execution Result

. Enable the QEMU virtualization daemon (virtqemud) and start it immediately.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ sudo systemctl enable virtqemud.service --now
----

===== Bridge Preparation for VM Connection

As an example, the pattern of reassigning the IP information assigned to the physical interface eno1 for the Manager NW to br0 is shown.

. Using the network configuration tool nmcli, delete the connection profile named eno1. Note: Be careful, as SSH connection to the relevant IF address will be disconnected. +
  (Configuration via iDRAC, etc. is recommended.)
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ sudo nmcli connection delete eno1
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
Connection eno1 (9c92fad9-6ecb-3e6c-eb4d-8a47c6f50c04) successfully deleted.
----

.Verification Steps
[source,console]
----
$ nmcli connection show
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
(Verify that eno1 does not exist)
----
--

. Using nmcli, create a bridge connection named br0 and configure it as a logical interface.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ sudo nmcli connection add type bridge con-name br0 ifname br0
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
Connection 'br0' (a2e9dcf9-7272-4877-b2de-2ab147668605) successfully added.
----

.Verification Steps
[source,console]
----
$ nmcli connection show
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
(Verify that br0 exists)
----
--

. Disable the Spanning Tree Protocol (STP) for bridge connection br0.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ sudo nmcli connection modify br0 bridge.stp no
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
None in particular
----

.Verification Steps
[source,console]
----
$ nmcli -f bridge.stp connection show br0
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
bridge.stp:                             no
----
--

. Manually configure a static IP address, gateway, and DNS for bridge br0. +
  This is done by reassigning what was originally assigned to eno1.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ sudo nmcli connection modify br0 ipv4.method manual ipv4.addresses \
  172.28.193.16/24 ipv4.gateway 172.28.193.251 ipv4.dns 8.8.8.8,8.8.4.4
($ sudo nmcli connection modify br0 ipv4.method manual ipv4.addresses \
   <MGR-IPAddr-VMHost> ipv4.gateway <MGR-GW-VMHost> ipv4.dns <DNS-SV-IPAddr>)
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
None in particular
----

.Verification Steps
[source,console]
----
$ nmcli -f ipv4.method,ipv4.addresses,ipv4.gateway,ipv4.dns connection show br0
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
ipv4.method:                            manual
ipv4.addresses:                         172.28.193.16/24
ipv4.gateway:                           172.28.193.251
ipv4.dns:                               8.8.8.8,8.8.4.4
----
--

. Add physical interface eno1 as bridge port br0-port1 of controller br0.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ sudo nmcli connection add type ethernet port-type bridge con-name br0-port1 ifname eno1 controller br0
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
Connection 'br0-port1' (1861e376-ea5b-414e-acd3-ccc90b859d0c) successfully added.
----

.Verification Steps
[source,console]
----
$ nmcli -f connection.interface-name,connection.master connection show br0-port1
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
connection.interface-name:              eno1
connection.master:                      br0
----
--

※ The following is to prevent DHCP address acquisition.

. Restart the NetworkManager service and apply the network configuration changes.
+
--

$ sudo systemctl restart NetworkManager
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
None in particular
----

[source,console]
----
None in particular
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
None in particular
----

===== KVM Guest Image Placement (Used by Cloud-init)

. Copy the RHEL 9.6 (<RHEL Version>) KVM image file to the image directory managed by libvirt.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ sudo cp rhel-9.6-x86_64-kvm.qcow2 /var/lib/libvirt/images/
($ sudo cp rhel-<RHEL Version>-x86_64-kvm.qcow2 /var/lib/libvirt/images/)
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
None in particular
----

.Verification Steps
[source,console]
----
$ sudo ls /var/lib/libvirt/images/rhel-9.6-x86_64-kvm.qcow2
($ sudo ls /var/lib/libvirt/images/rhel-<RHEL Version>-x86_64-kvm.qcow2)
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
/var/lib/libvirt/images/rhel-9.6-x86_64-kvm.qcow2
----
--

===== VM Creation Script (create-vm.sh) Editing
Refer to the following for the script content.

[source,bash]
----
#!/bin/bash

case $1 in
  "d1-f01-k8s-master01 ")       ★VM name to specify as argument = VMNAME
    VMNAME=d1-f01-k8s-master01  ★VM name
    VCPUS=8                     ★Number of CPUs to assign to the VM
    RAMSIZE=16                  ★Memory size to assign to the VM (Unit: GB)
    DISKSIZE=100                ★Disk size to assign to the VM (Unit: GB)
    IPADDR=172.28.193.10        ★ VM Manager NW IP address
    ;;
  "d1-f01-k8s-master02 ")       ★When creating multiple VMs, edit and add entries the same way as the first one
    VMNAME=d1-f01-k8s-master02  ★
    VCPUS=4                     ★
    RAMSIZE=16                  ★
    DISKSIZE=100                ★
    IPADDR=172.28.193.11        ★
esac

# Directory where image files will be stored
IMAGE_DIR=/var/lib/libvirt/images
# VM image file name
IMAGE_FILE=${VMNAME}.qcow2
# Base image file name (must exist before running the script)
BASE_FILE=rhel-9.6-x86_64-kvm.qcow2

CIDATA_PATH=$IMAGE_DIR/cidata-${VMNAME}.iso
#CIDATA_PATH=$PWD/cidata-${VMNAME}.iso
IMAGE_PATH=$IMAGE_DIR/$IMAGE_FILE
BASE_PATH=$IMAGE_DIR/$BASE_FILE

echo "VMNAME ........ $VMNAME"
echo "VCPUS ......... $VCPUS"
echo "RAMSIZE ....... $RAMSIZE G"
echo "DISKSIZE ...... $DISKSIZE G"
echo "IPADDR ........ $IPADDR"
echo "CIDATA_PATH ... $CIDATA_PATH"
echo "IMAGE_PATH .... $IMAGE_PATH"
echo "BASE_PATH ..... $BASE_PATH"
echo
read -p "Is this OK? ('y' to proceed) " ANS
if [ "$ANS" != "y" -a "$ANS" != "Y" ]; then
  echo "Aborted"
  exit 1
fi

# Go to the image dir
pushd $IMAGE_DIR

# Check if the base image exists
if sudo [ ! -r "$BASE_FILE" ]; then
  echo "error: base image file $BASE_PATH not found."
  exit 1
fi

# Check if the VM image already exists
# if not, create the new one
if sudo [ ! -f "$IMAGE_FILE" ]; then
  echo "info: image file $IMAGE_PATH not found. creating new image"
  sudo qemu-img create -f qcow2 -F qcow2 -b "$BASE_PATH" "$IMAGE_FILE" ${DISKSIZE}G
  if [ $? -ne 0 ]; then
    echo "error: failed to create image"
    exit 1
  fi
fi

# Dump the image info
sudo qemu-img info "$IMAGE_FILE"

popd

# Check if the cloud-init data file exists
# if not, generate it
if [ ! -r $CIDATA_PATH ]; then
  mkdir -p $VMNAME
  cd $VMNAME

  cat > user-data <<-EOS
#cloud-config
password: <Password>            ★Login password (default login user is cloud-user)
chpasswd: {expire: False}
ssh_pwauth: True
timezone: "Asia/Tokyo"
EOS

  cat > network-config << EOS
network:
  version: 1
  config:
    - type: physical
      name: eth1
      subnets:
        - type: static
          address: $IPADDR/24
          gateway: 172.28.193.251 # ★Default gateway IP address
EOS

  cat > meta-data <<-EOS
instance-id: $VMNAME
local-hostname: $VMNAME
EOS

  genisoimage -output "$CIDATA_PATH" -volid cidata -joliet -rock user-data meta-data network-config
  cd ..
fi

sudo virt-install \
  --name $VMNAME \
  --ram $(($RAMSIZE * 1024)) \
  --vcpus $VCPUS \
  --os-variant rhel9.0 \
  --arch x86_64 \
  --virt-type kvm \
  --boot hd \
  --disk $IMAGE_PATH,device=disk,bus=virtio,format=qcow2 \
  --disk $CIDATA_PATH,device=cdrom \
  --network network:default \
  --network bridge:br0 \
  --graphics none \
  --import
----

※ Edit the ★-marked fields (VM name, number of CPUs, memory size, disk size, Manager NW IP address, password, default gateway) according to the required resources and NW configuration. Refer to the following as a guide for CPU, memory, and disk.

* CPU: 4 (8 if possible)
* Memory: 16  ※ Unit: GB
* Disk: 100  ※ Unit: GB

===== VM Creation Script (create-vm.sh) Execution

. Execute the script named create-vm.sh to create a virtual machine with the specified name.
+

.Execution Steps

$ sudo ./create-vm.sh <VM名>

※ Press Ctrl + ] to exit

===== Post-VM Creation Tasks

When a VM is created using create-vm.sh, the default route of the VM with RHEL installed is set to prioritize eth0; change it to eth1. That is, delete the default route and DNS pointing to the NAT side, and change to the bridge-side IF.

CAUTION: Without performing the following procedure, the server cannot be directly accessed from outside the Manager NW subnet.

. Verify current routing information.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ ip route
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
default via 192.168.122.1 dev eth0 proto dhcp src 192.168.122.176 metric 100
default via 192.168.2.2 dev eth1 proto static metric 101
192.168.2.0/24 dev eth1 proto kernel scope link src 192.168.2.127 metric 101
192.168.122.0/24 dev eth0 proto kernel scope link src 192.168.122.176 metric 100
----

.Verification Steps
[source,console]
----
(Does the execution result output contain a default route pointing to eth0?)
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
(The execution result output should contain a default route pointing to eth0)
----
--

. Verify current DNS information.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ more /etc/resolv.conf
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
# Generated by NetworkManager
nameserver 192.168.122.1
----

.Verification Steps
[source,console]
----
(Is the nameserver in the execution result 192.168.122.1?)
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
(The nameserver in the execution result should be 192.168.122.1)
----
--

==== VM Image Directory Relocation and Mounting

On the VM host, with the default partitioning (※), the capacity of "/" is small and VM images can fill the disk; relocate the VM image directory and mount it. +
※ Configuring partitions during OS installation is difficult, so default partitioning is used.

===== Creation of Target Directory

. Set SELinux to Permissive mode (warnings only) to temporarily disable it.

=== RHEL Subscription Application (Removal)

==== RHEL Subscription Application

After OS installation, for license activation, connect the installed server to Red Hat Hybrid Cloud Console using RHC (Remote Host Configuration) and apply the subscription using an activation key. +
(For VMs, apply the subscription after VM creation.) +
RHC is a client tool that integrates subscription registration of RHEL systems and enabling of Red Hat Insights.

Note: Going forward, work is performed as the user "cloud-user".

. Using the activation key, connect the Red Hat system to RHC and apply the subscription.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ sudo rhc connect --activation-key <RHC-activation-key> --organization <RHC-organization>
----

.Execution Result
[source,console]
----
Connecting <server> to Red Hat.
This might take a few seconds.

● Connected to Red Hat Subscription Management
● Connected to Red Hat Insights
● Activated the Remote Host Configuration daemon

Successfully connected to Red Hat!

Manage your connected systems: https://red.ht/connector
----

.Verification Steps

----
$ sudo rhc status
----

.Verification Result
[source,console]
----
Connection status for <server>:

● Connected to Red Hat Subscription Management
● Connected to Red Hat Insights
● The Remote Host Configuration daemon is active

Manage your connected systems: https://red.ht/connector
----
--

==== RHEL Subscription Removal

Note: When uninstalling the OS, execute the following command to remove the subscription.

. Disconnect the Red Hat system from RHC and remove it from management.
+
--

----
$ sudo rhc disconnect

=== cloud-init Procedures

==== What is cloud-init
cloud-init is an initial configuration mechanism executed at the first OS boot. +
After OS deployment is complete via MAAS, it runs automatically at OS startup to execute commands, create files, and perform various configuration tasks.

MAAS and cloud-init divide their roles in the OS deployment process as follows.

* MAAS
** Responsible for OS deployment to physical servers.
** Specifies user_data for cloud-init during OS deployment.
** Passes the user_data to the OS without interpreting its contents.
+
* cloud-init
** Runs automatically at the first OS boot.
** Interprets user_data passed from MAAS.
** Executes the commands and settings described in user_data on the OS.

In this way, MAAS serves as the trigger for launching cloud-init, and the actual initial construction processing is the responsibility of cloud-init.

==== cloud-init Log Verification
For verifying cloud-init operation or investigating issues, check the following logs and files.

* Execution log
** /var/log/cloud-init-output.log
*** Records the standard output and standard error output of commands executed by cloud-init.

* user-data verification
** /var/lib/cloud/instance/user-data.txt
*** Stores the user_data executed by cloud-init.
*** Same as the user_data specified during MAAS OS deployment.

==== cloud-init Re-execution Procedure
Select the target Node in the MAAS GUI. +
Execute "Release..." from the Actions menu to change the status from Deployed to Ready. +
Select "Deploy..." from the Actions menu. +
Check "Cloud-init user-data..." and paste user_data to execute.
--

=== (Reference) NFS Server Construction

For reference, the NFS server construction procedure is shown. For reference purposes only, only Execution Steps are shown.

. Install the NFS server. (Skip if already installed.)
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ sudo dnf -y install nfs-utils
----
--

. Create the export directory / Set SELinux context. Create the directory to share via NFS and configure appropriate permissions.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ sudo mkdir -p /export/nfs
$ sudo chmod 1777 /export/nfs
$ sudo semanage fcontext -a -t nfs_t "/export/nfs(/.*)?"
$ sudo restorecon -Rv /export/nfs
----
--

. Add export settings. Add the following line to /etc/exports to configure the NFS server to export the directory.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ echo "/export/nfs *(rw,async,no_root_squash)" | sudo tee -a /etc/exports
----
--

. Restart the NFS server to apply the settings.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ sudo systemctl enable nfs-server
$ sudo systemctl start nfs-server
----
--

. Create a file for encryption. ※ The following is a 10GB empty disk image file, but a larger disk capacity is recommended.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ sudo mkdir -p /secure-nfs
$ sudo dd if=/dev/zero of=/secure-nfs/nfs.img bs=1M count=512000 status=progress
9471787008 bytes (9.5 GB, 8.8 GiB) copied, 3 s, 3.2 GB/s
10240+0 records in
10240+0 records out
10737418240 bytes (11 GB, 10 GiB) copied, 3.39918 s, 3.2 GB/s
----
--

. Configure encryption with LUKS. Set the passphrase here.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ sudo dnf -y install cryptsetup
$ sudo cryptsetup luksFormat /secure-nfs/nfs.img
Are you sure? (Type 'yes' in capital letters): YES #Type "YES" in uppercase
Enter passphrase for /secure-nfs/nfs.img:          #Remember the passphrase.
Verify passphrase:
----
--

. Open (map) the encrypted device. /dev/mapper/secure_nfs is created.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ sudo cryptsetup luksOpen /secure-nfs/nfs.img secure_nfs
Enter passphrase for /secure-nfs/nfs.img:  #Enter the passphrase set above.
※ The following warning appears but can be ignored.
Warning: keyslot operation could fail as it requires more than available memory.
$ ls /dev/mapper/secure_nfs
/dev/mapper/secure_nfs
----
--

. Create a filesystem.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ sudo mkfs.ext4 /dev/mapper/secure_nfs
mke2fs 1.46.5 (30-Dec-2021)
Creating filesystem with 131067904 4k blocks and 32768000 inodes
Filesystem UUID: 3993920a-118d-48d1-8a93-0cb07d280936
Superblock backups stored on blocks:
        32768, 98304, 163840, 229376, 294912, 819200, 884736, 1605632, 2654208,
        4096000, 7962624, 11239424, 20480000, 23887872, 71663616, 78675968,
102400000

Allocating group tables: done
Writing inode tables: done
Creating journal (262144 blocks): done
Writing superblocks and filesystem accounting information: done
----
--

. Mount the encrypted volume to the mount point.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ sudo mount /dev/mapper/secure_nfs /export/nfs
$ sudo restorecon -Rv /export/nfs
----
--

. After applying the settings, restart the service.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ sudo exportfs -ra
$ sudo systemctl restart nfs-server
----
--

. Add to /etc/crypttab and /etc/fstab for automatic mounting at startup.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ echo "secure_nfs /secure-nfs/nfs.img none luks" | sudo tee -a /etc/crypttab
$ echo "/dev/mapper/secure_nfs /export/nfs ext4 defaults 0 0" | sudo tee -a /etc/fstab
----
--

. Verify that the device is configured for encryption.
+
※ Verify that /dev/mapper/secure_nfs is mounted to /export/nfs.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ lsblk -f
NAME         FSTYPE      FSVER            LABEL  UUID                                 FSAVAIL FSUSE% MOUNTPOINTS
loop0        crypto_LUKS 2                       eb818d7f-302b-4447-b080-5972ddc4bc4a
mqsecure_nfs ext4        1.0                     3993920a-118d-48d1-8a93-0cb07d280936  466.1G     0% /export/nfs
sr0          iso9660     Joliet Extension cidata 2026-05-08-03-04-37-00
vda
tqvda1
tqvda2       vfat        FAT16                   7B77-95E7                             192.4M     4% /boot/efi
tqvda3       xfs                          boot   f96270a1-6b74-4ed5-b08e-f7cd1fa50ebc  664.1M    31% /boot
mqvda4       xfs                          root   b977dbef-b985-4f0d-8c41-0a34281d3838  489.5G    51% /

$ df -h /export/nfs
Filesystem              Size  Used Avail Use% Mounted on
/dev/mapper/secure_nfs  492G   28K  467G   1% /export/nfs
$ mount | grep secure_nfs
/dev/mapper/secure_nfs on /export/nfs type ext4 (rw,relatime,seclabel)
----
--
+
※ Display metadata information of the encrypted device.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ sudo cryptsetup luksDump /secure-nfs/nfs.img
LUKS header information
Version:        2
Epoch:          3
Metadata area:  16384 [bytes]
Keyslots area:  16744448 [bytes]
UUID:           eb818d7f-302b-4447-b080-5972ddc4bc4a
    :
    (omitted)
    :
----
--
+
※ Verify the current state of the encrypted device.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ sudo cryptsetup status secure_nfs
  type:    LUKS2
  cipher:  aes-xts-plain64
  keysize: 512 bits
  key location: keyring
  device:  /dev/loop0
  loop:    /secure-nfs/nfs.img
  sector size:  4096
  offset:  32768 sectors

=== (Reference) Backup Storage (MinIO) Construction

For reference, the backup storage (MinIO) construction procedure is shown. For reference purposes only, only Execution Steps are shown.

Prerequisites: docker and go must be installed. +
Prerequisites: Create a working directory and work there. Container image creation should be performed only in the directory where go is installed. +
Prerequisites: Encrypt the backup storage and operate HashiCorp Vault + KES + MinIO with SSE-KMS.

. Create a bridge for containers (to connect Vault/KES/MinIO).
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ sudo docker network create kes-net
----
--

. Build the MinIO container image.
+
※ The executable generated by "go install" is placed in one of the following: +
※ $GOBIN if GOBIN is set +
※ $GOPATH/bin if GOPATH is set +
※ Otherwise ~/go/bin (default) ★ This is assumed.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ env CGO_ENABLED=0 GOOS=linux GOARCH=amd64 \
  go install github.com/minio/minio@RELEASE.2025-10-15T17-29-55Z
----
--

. Add the user to the group.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ sudo useradd minio-user -s /sbin/nologin
----
--

. Prepare the working directory.
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ mkdir ./minio
----
--

. Create the container image.
+
※ Move the minio binary to the directory. (Move to the directory containing the executable generated by "go install" and create a Dockerfile.)
+
--
.Execution Steps
[source,console]
----
$ cd ~/go/bin
$ vi Dockerfile
# Dockerfile
FROM alpine:latest
RUN apk --no-cache add ca-certificates
COPY ./minio /usr/local/bin/minio
RUN chmod +x /usr/local/bin/minio
EXPOSE 9000 9001
ENTRYPOINT ["/usr/local/bin/minio"]

$ sudo docker build -t my-minio:latest .

 => => transferring dockerfile: 332B                                                                                                                                                    0.0s

 => [auth] library/alpine:pull token for registry-1.docker.io                                                                                                                           0.0s
 => [internal] load .dockerignore                                                                                                                                                       0.0s
 => => transferring context: 2B                                                                                                                                                         0.0s
